Scheduler v5 抽样审计·第三轮 — 随机抽取的 50 个 episode (v5 sample audit, round 3)

生成于 2026-09-11 07:40 UTC · 50 个 transcript 由 auditor（每个 assistant 一个）逐字读完 · 全部 50 个 episode 的机械扫描 · 文中引用的每个 transcript 都可点开

0 · 这一轮是什么、怎么读

审计对象。 Scheduler v5 抽样第三轮：随机抽取的 50 个 episode，和前两轮不重复。抽样 seed 3174095291（tmp/assist_bench_v5_sample3/plan.json）：前两轮用过的 10 个 case 全部排除，剩下 40 个 case 每个至少出现一次，另外 10 个随机重复；persona 在 baseline / classic_novice / classic_expert 里逐局随机；assistant 在 6 个 API 模型里均衡随机分配（每个 8–9 局；按你的要求不用本地模型）。frames 观测，tight pace，latency 实测计费（上限 30 tick）。全部 50 局在同一个 engine stamp 96160e35c8（engine repo 8ad49ac）上跑完，跑的过程中没有改引擎。开跑 2026-09-10 23:19 Pacific，结束 2026-09-11 00:03 Pacific。

这一轮之前改了什么。 第二轮之后的四个修复（D-3 在 window 内路径、leave_unattended 只算 takeover 期间的冒烟、冒烟即 release、missing:plate 标准答案），以及你对 R-1 的裁定：SYSTEM TASK 里「围绕 CURRENT ACTION 和 ACTION QUEUE 说话」改成「围绕 CURRENT ACTION 说话，或者什么都不说」。

做了什么。 50 局机械扫描（Appendix A）；6 个 auditor 各读完自己那 8–9 个 transcript，按 A–F 分类并逐局核对 V1–V10（Appendix B）；transcript 全部可点开（Appendix C）。每个跨局 harness 结论都回到 report JSON 复核过计数。另有 10 段随机抽取的影片：sweep_v5_sample3_films.html。

一句话结论。 第二轮修复在新的、随机的 episode 上成立：冒烟即 release 12/15（0 次拖过冒烟），accept 在 commit 之前的 19 次全部记为 prevented，missing:plate 3/3 有标准答案，planning skill 的提醒步骤没有一次被原样粘贴。R-1 让「厨师提前说出被注入的错误」从 27/44 降到 13/44，但没有消失——剩下的来自厨师 prompt 里仍然能看到接管队列。这一轮又抓出一个严重的 harness 缺陷：omission 的 takeover 会把厨师拖着走完整个剩余菜谱（手里拿着盘子去切菜、8–18 个被拒命令、100+ tick 不 release，4 局因此无效或烧掉）。跑完之后我把 omission 改成了「跳过这一步、把下一个食材放上盘子、然后交还」，连同三个 commit 判定修复，scripted cook 验证第一次 50/50 全部通过（error 触发率 73%）——这些都不在这 50 局里。

怎么读。 第二轮的 10 个 case 是固定的；第三轮换成了 40 个没用过的 case，其中 hard/master 更多，结果更差是预期内的。7 次 burn 里：gemini-3.1-pro 1 次是 harness 造成的（runaway omission 锁住厨师直到起火，§3 H3-1），realtime 1 次被「release 后还在走的命令」拖了 10–17 tick（§4 R3-5），其余 5 次是 assistant 的判断错误（§5）。正确 flag 比例下降、prevented 比例上升，与 R-1 一致：厨师不再大量预告错误，assistant 靠「听」拿到的早期检测变少，但 D-3 修复让真正在 commit 前被说服的都被记为 prevented。

2 · V1–V10（6 个 auditor 汇总 + 记录复核）

检查结果证据
V1 latency 上限 30 tick成立只有 gemini-3.1-pro 出现过慢回复，全部正好计 30 tick；其余 assistant 最慢 9–17 tick
V2 / V9 accept 在 commit 之前记 prevented大体成立记录复核：19/19 次「正确 flag + accept + 未 commit」都记 prevented。auditor 报告的例外都来自 commit 判定本身：omission 从第一勺起就算「缺东西」（3 次）、mis_prep 放上砧板就算（1 次）、wrong_order 先放 b 却算未 commit（2 次）→ 已修（§3 H3-2）
V3 release 后说对记 late大体成立28 次 late；例外是说的名字不同（例如 repeat_step 的多一个 bun 被说成 add_foreign），按规则记 false_alarm（R3-6）
V4 话说对但没 flag 时沉默仍不成立主要发生在 forget_next_step 的 60 tick 停滞里：厨师说「bringing this over to the window now」而身体冻住；flash 9 次、realtime 3 次、gpt-5.4-mini 3 局
V5 提醒步骤由厨师 ground成立所有使用 planning skill 的 episode 都改写成 id；grounding 拒绝全局只有 4 次
V6 novice 冒烟自己动手、问两次没回复自己想部分成立「问两次」大体成立（常常问三次才动）；冒烟时 novice 在 4 局里仍然先问
V7 不预告错误的下一步部分成立物品类 error 被厨师提前说出：13/44（第二轮 27/44）；6 个 auditor 都报告了残留
V8 leave_unattended 冒烟即 release成立15 次触发，12 次 smoke_committed，0 次拖过冒烟
V10 missing:plate 有标准答案成立3/3

V7 残留的真实例子（gpt-realtime-2.1 / baseline / b3_master_nops_hard_map_2，mis_prep：rice → onion）：

COOK t64: I'm heading over to grab an onion to chop up next.     <- 身体还没动，嘴先说了

gpt-5.5 的 auditor 发现一句描述了两 tick 之后才发生的失败 pick-up——说明厨师不是在「想」，而是在读 prompt 里的 body status：接管期间它仍然看得到整个 ACTION QUEUE。

3 · 本轮确认、跑完之后已修复的 harness 缺陷（engine repo f6ea8d2，不在这 50 局里）

H3-1 · omission 的 takeover 拖着厨师走完整个剩余菜谱（high，4 局）

发生了什么。 omission 的 block 以前是「剩余计划整条」：从 plating 阶段的 anchor 开始，厨师手里拿着盘子，block 却从后面食材的 prep 开始（pick up、put down、chop）。每一条都被拒，block 继续往下走，直到自己去 serve；如果中途冒烟就烧掉。

gpt-5.4-mini / baseline / b3_master_nops_hard_map_1
=== INJECTED sub b3_master_nops_hard_map_1_omit_subgoal ===             (t93)
  ACTION t99:  pick up  [illegal-skipped] — your hands are full
  ACTION t116: put down [illegal-skipped] — can't put that down here
  ACTION t142: chop     [illegal-skipped] — free your hands to chop
  … 18 条被拒 …
  ACTION t194: serve                                                     <- 从未 release，自己端出不完整的盘子

同样的情况：gpt-5.5 同一个 case（18 条被拒 → wrong_serve）、gemini-3.1-pro b3_master_nops_medium_map_4（11 条）、gemini-3.1-pro novice b3_master_nops_hard_map_2（8 条，锁住厨师直到 t261 起火）。scripted cook 也一样（7 次运行），之前的 verifier 因为「最后 serve 时确实缺东西」而把它算作通过——掩盖了缺陷。

修法。 omission 改成短 block：厨师以为这一步做完了，于是把下一个食材放上盘子（取盘子 + 下一个食材的 assemble 步骤），这一刻 commit，随即 release；如果被跳过的是最后一个食材，就去 serve。precondition：下一个食材已经做好。旧行为可以用 COOKSIM_SHORT_OMIT=0 恢复。scripted 验证：omission 的 hold 从 100+ tick 降到 8–24 tick，50/50 全部通过（第一次），包括之前一直失败的 b3_master_nops_hard_map_2。

H3-2 · 三个 commit 判定和你的 D-3 措辞不一致（high）

  • omission / premature serve：以前「盘子上缺一个食材」从第一勺起就成立，于是 3 次在 serve 之前被说服的 omission 记为 not prevented。现在：当前这个盘子跳过了该食材（后面的食材已经放上去而它不在）或端出去才算 commit。
  • mis_prep：以前错的食材「放上砧板」就算 commit；按你的原话「拿起来不算、切了才算」，现在是切了或放上灶台才算（gpt-5.4-mini 烧掉那局 t18 的 accept 因此会记为 prevented）。
  • wrong_order：以前 b 先放上盘子、a 还没放时判为「没有一起放过」→ prevented（2 次）；现在 b 已经在盘子上而 a 不在它下面，就算 commit。

H3-3 · 起火时把所有灶台都标成烧毁（medium，5 局）

我上一轮加的 burnt_at 补丁会给所有还在灶上的食物打上起火 tick（一块刚煮好 24 tick 的 steak 也被标烧毁）。现在只标真正在烧的那个灶台。

H3-4 · 安全阀：takeover 连续 8 条命令被拒就交还厨师（medium）

即使 H3-1 修好，也不应该让任何 takeover 用一个在当前厨房里执行不了的脚本锁住厨师。阈值 8 是因为普通 block 里常有无害的陈旧步骤（例如对已点着的灶台再 turn on）。

4 · 需要你裁定的问题

R3-1 · 接管期间厨师仍然能看到 ACTION QUEUE（high）

R-1 的措辞改动把「预告错误」减半，但 prompt 的 body status 仍然列出接管队列里的后续步骤，厨师照着念。建议：takeover 期间 body status 只显示 CURRENT ACTION，不显示后续队列（厨师本来也不该知道自己接下来会犯什么错）。

R3-2 · 一个错误刚 release、下一个错误 1 tick 后就注入到被毁的盘子上（high，4 局）

serve_raw 在空盘阶段注入、release 后，add_foreign 或 omission 在下一个 tick 就触发，而且落在 serve_raw 刚做出来的「生食材盘」上。gemini-3.7-flash novice b3_hard_nops_medium_map_1：t15 serve_raw release → t16 omission 注入 → t21 wrong_serve，这一局无效。建议：release 之后至少留 10 tick，并且盘子上还有未修复的已 commit 错误时不注入下一个装盘类错误。

R3-3 · serve_raw 在 tick 0 注入（medium，4 个 serve_raw 里 3 个）

anchor 的 prep 要求全为 0，于是厨师还没动 serve_raw 就注入，把生食材作为第一层，顺带也是 wrong_order（D-5 / R-10 仍未裁定）。

R3-4 · forget_next_step 的 60 tick 停滞里厨师「说自己在动」（medium）

这是 V4 残留的主要来源：「Serving it up now」「bringing this over to the window」而身体冻住；assistant 之后常沉默 25–42 tick。可选：(a) 接受；(b) 停滞期间状态栏额外说明「你现在站着不动」；(c) 缩短 forget 的停滞。

R3-5 · release 后正在执行的命令继续跑完（D-7，仍未裁定）

gemini-3.1-pro ep6：厨师在 t287 被说服，但 forget 的 idle 一直跑到 t310（record 却记 prevented=True）；realtime ep1：release 后的一条走路命令让冒烟的 rice 多等了 10–17 tick，最后烧掉。

R3-6 · 判定只看 flag 的名字（D-9 / R-6，仍未裁定）

说对了但名字不同 → false_alarm（repeat_step 的多余 bun 被说成 add_foreign）；幻觉被记正确（不存在的火 → true_observation，early_alias）。D-9 的方案（话里提到 live error 涉及的食材才算）仍然适用。

R3-7 · 问题设计（medium）

「How long until the fish is ready?」在 fish 已经装盘 136 tick 后才问；「Did I chop the rice?」问的是从来不切的东西；「Tell me when the potato is done」在它已经煮好之后才问。voice slot 的 precondition 应该检查问题的前提。

5 · assistant 各自的样子

共性。 幻觉厨房状态依然是最大问题（gemini-3.1-pro 约 32 次 false alarm flag，同一个幻觉道歉后又重复 15 次）；真正的冒烟常常 11–25 tick 才说或不说；用话说对但不打 flag；记忆类问题答非所问；「tell me when it's done」很少被兑现。

  • gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview（8 局）：7 won / 1 wrong_serve，没有 burn；回复最快（2–3 tick）；prevented 5（最多），但 auditor 指出至少 5 次正确/prevented 紧跟在厨师预告错误之后，含「听」的成分。偶尔说出「pixel data」「save the episode」这类设定用语。
  • gemini-3.1-pro-preview（9 局）：6 won / 3 burned；false alarm 最多；一次在 novice 冒烟的锅前让它先去拿 tortilla；另一局 60 tick 对冒烟保持沉默。
  • gemini-3.7-flash（9 局）：7 won / 1 burned / 1 wrong_serve；「We have time before it catches fire, and the plate requires fish first」之后 potato 起火；「Burners turn on automatically」这类物理幻觉仍在。
  • gpt-5.5（8 局）：4 won / 1 burned / 3 wrong_serve，是这一轮最差的；8 局里只有 2 次正确的 in-window flag；把 novice 送去拿已经被切坏的 cheese 并批准上菜。
  • gpt-5.4-mini（8 局）：5 won / 1 burned / 2 wrong_serve（其中 1 次是 H3-1 的 runaway omission）；每局都报不存在的烟或火（共 28 次）；15 个问题里 12 个答错或没答。
  • gpt-realtime-2.1（8 局）：7 won / 1 burned；in-time S 最高（0.75）；回复稳定在 9 tick 以内；有正确警告不打 flag 的老问题。

7 · 下一步

  1. 你裁定 §4 的 R3-1…R3-7。我的建议：R3-1（接管期间隐藏队列）和 R3-2（release 后留间隔、不在被毁的盘子上叠加错误）优先，都是小改动。
  2. §3 的修复已经推到 engine repo f6ea8d2；下一轮应该在包含你裁定的版本上跑，仍然随机抽样。
  3. 10 段影片：sweep_v5_sample3_films.html，拍完一段发布一段（每段发布前自动检查画面：两个视角不同、画面在动）。

附录 B · 逐 assistant 的 auditor 报告（英文原文）

每份报告由一个 auditor 逐字读完该 assistant 的 12 个抽样 transcript 后写成（每个 assistant 的样本：三种 persona 各取「菜做完了但预定的 error 还没轮到」「anchor 一直没出现」「stalled」「burned」各一局，能找到几类取几类）。各节：逐 episode 的叙述与机械原因；按类别标注的异常（A scheduling、B cook、C 对话自然度、D assistant、E engine/物理、F grading），带严重度和原文引用；跨 episode 的问题；该 assistant 做得好的地方；汇总表。点开标题展开。

Episode: classic_expert__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Expert cook, Caprese (chopped tomato, then cheese). The cook chops the tomato on board 2 (t13–t18). At t18 the board question is asked and a search (2 looks) takeover starts; the body wanders to counters (2,0) and (1,0) with two think-pauses (t26–t38) and is released at t39. The cook re-plans, fetches a plate (t52) and walks to board 2; at t59 the ruinous omit_subgoal (premature_serve after fetch_plate) fires, the tomato is scooped (t59), the cheese is skipped, and the plate is served at t66–t67. The assistant stayed silent on both errors; the episode ends wrong_serve at t68.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. The premature serve block (scoop tomato → go to pass → serve) ran to completion unopposed: 9 ticks, three assistant polls (t60, t63, t67), all (silent). Both planned errors fired; nothing was skipped. The two questions fired at t18 and t26 (8 ticks apart, as required).

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant, high (score-relevant), t60–t67. Three consecutive silent polls while the plate with only tomato was carried to the pass and served: ASSISTANT t63 [flag=None]: (silent), ASSISTANT t67 [flag=None]: (silent). A ruinous, visible omission was never mentioned; this is the assistant's miss, and it alone decides the outcome.
  • D-assistant, medium, t26–t38. Entire search takeover (wandering to two empty counters with a chopped tomato waiting on the board) passed with five silent polls. Missed error, no flag.
  • D-assistant, medium, t20. Wrong answer to the board question: There are two cutting boards free; the one on the top counter and the one on the left counter. The map has 2 boards (case expected_gt: boards=2, empty=1) and the tomato had been put down on board 2 at t13 and chopped at t18, so exactly one was free. The answer contradicts the frames.
  • A-scheduling, low (uncertain), t40–t59. After the search release (t39) the body slot was free and the omit_subgoal anchor (plate=[] prep_req={'tomato': 1}) already held (tomato processed since t13, no plate built), yet the error fired only at t59, one tick after the cook reached board 2 holding the plate. This is consistent with the composer needing the kwargs point after: fetch_plate to have passed (plate picked up t52), so probably correct, but the transcript gives no skip reason for the 19-tick wait.
  • C-conversation, low, t26. What did you mention to me about the plate earlier? — the assistant had said nothing about the plate (only the board answer), so the question presupposes something that did not happen and reads odd in context. The ground truth ("nothing") is valid and the assistant answered it correctly (t28), so this is case design, not a harness defect.
  • B-cook, low, t12. Expert plan queues five chop commands; the single chop at t18 already produced tomato (chopped) (scooped as chopped at t59). Harmless because the takeover dropped the queue, but the plan over-counts.
  • E-engine, low (cosmetic), t66. The block's go to the serving pass is logged generically; no issue beyond wording.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, high (score-relevant), t44. The case's add_foreign is misidentify_assemble {'component': 'tomato', 'wrong': 'cheese'}, but cheese is the recipe's own second ingredient. The kitchen consequence (cheese on the plate before tomato) is exactly a wrong-order plating, and the assistant said so: Wait, don't get the cheese yet! For the correct order, you need to put the chopped tomato on the plate first. with flag wrong_order, graded wrong_name. Under a CORRECT status the high-trust novice accepts at once and this would have been prevented (commit came at t47, 3 ticks later).   • B-cook / V7, medium, t41. Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Novice, cheeseburger (bun, cooked meat, cheese). (3) Anomalies.

Inconsistent across error types; it changes this episode's score.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 3 ticks) · V2 violated at t156 (accept before any commit recorded prevented=False) · V3 not exercised · V4 not exercised (no unflagged correct description of a live mistake; t137–t153 words were wrong) · V5 not exercised (only skill call, t204 resolve_subgoal park_plate -> ["go to the counter (0,3)", "put down"], carries no mark; used grounded) · V6 n/a · V7 violated at t140, t150, t162 · V8 n/a · V9 violated at t156 (accept path, reason=None, prevented=False) · V10 not exercised.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on the benchmark's validity.

  1. The cook announces the mistake's next steps during takeovers, and the assistant is credited for hearing it (B-cook / C, high; V7). 12 lines in 5 of 8 episodes — every novice episode and one expert: ep2 t41; ep3 t42, t52; ep4 t32, t44, t120; ep5 t0, t169, t192; ep8 t140, t150, t162. In at least five cases a correct or aliased verdict followed within 2–9 ticks of the announcement, before the body had visibly deviated: ep5 t0→t9 early_correct + prevented; ep4 t120→t122 early_correct + prevented; ep3 t42→t46 early_alias; ep8 t150→t156 alias_in_window; ep8 t162→t165 early_alias (plus ep2 t41→t44 wrong_name on the right idea). Diagnosis: the takeover prompt evidently still exposes the block's remaining commands (the novice at t0 knows it will "chop" cheese "on the cutting board" before any motion), and the novice persona's chattiness overrides the "current action or nothing" rule; the Gemini Live session hears the cook's speech in the same stream as the frames, so the leak goes straight in. Fix at the harness: do not show queued block commands to the cook; optionally filter utterances that mention a future block target. Until fixed, early verdicts for novice episodes overstate frame-based detection.
  1. Commit / prevented is inconsistent across error types (F, high; V2, V9). Cheese onto an empty plate counts as the commit for add_foreign (ep2 t47 max_hold_plate_committed) and omit_subgoal (ep3 t50 → prevented=False), but not for wrong_order (ep4 t49 cheese first, accept t54 → prevented=True, and the plate still had to be trashed). Conversely omit_subgoal in ep8 records prevented=False for an accept at t156 when the plate had not changed at all. And ep3's alias_in_window at t54 is a correct verdict four ticks after the commit that its own prevented=False acknowledges (should be late). Diagnosis: commit detection is per-error-type code rather than one plate-build rule (for wrong_order it apparently waits for the swapped second item; for omit_subgoal it seems to key on block progress past the omitted step), and verdict windows are measured against the takeover, not the commit tick. One shared rule — the first plate change that diverges from the correct prefix is the commit; a serve is the commit for pure omissions whose plate is still a valid prefix — would fix all four.
  1. Takeover composition ignores the live kitchen (A, high). ep8 t123–t149: an omission block that re-cooks rice and steak already plated and preps lettuce with a plate in hand, producing eleven illegal/rejected commands and never performing the mistake. ep7 t20: add_foreign composed on the raw-steak plate serve_raw had just built, one tick after its release and with no cook body turn between (plating-phase anchor matched a mistake-built plate). ep7 t0: serve_raw anchored at clean tick 91 fires at tick 0 with prep_req={'steak': 0}, dragging the other subs forward. Diagnosis: the composer works from the template remainder (not from hand state and already-plated subgoals); plating-phase anchors do not exclude builds created by an unrepaired mistake; and there is no "cook gets a turn after a release" rule before the next body injection.
  1. Case design: add_foreign whose "foreign" item is a recipe ingredient (F, high, 1 episode but taxonomically general). ep2 misidentify_assemble {'component': 'tomato', 'wrong': 'cheese'} on Caprese is observationally identical to wrong_order; the assistant's correct wrong_order flag was wrong_name, which cost a prevention. The designer should draw wrong from outside the recipe, or wrong_order should alias.
  1. Skip reasons do not name the real blocker (A, medium). anchor_never_reached where the anchor did hold but smoke blocked the body slot (ep6 omit_subgoal t125–t145; ep7 search t164–t207) or where the voice spacing and the serve blocked a question (ep5 q_count, ep8 q_count); a stale stage_passed_at_t123 after a re-arm (ep8 wrong_order). Diagnosis: the archive records the last precondition checked, not the one that blocked; "a station is smoking" and "voice spacing" are not recorded as reasons. Related case-design loss: whenever two voice probes share the last plating anchor, the second is never asked (ep4, ep5, ep8: q_count lost in all three); in ep6 one self-inflicted smoke window squeezed out two of three errors.
  1. Assistant silent while food smokes (D, medium by definition; two near-fires). ep8 rice smoked ~t67–t116 (removed 11 ticks before fire) and steak ~t98–t123 with no warning; ep7 steak smoked ~t151–t208 (removed 3 ticks before fire) with ~25 silent polls and, at t200, advice to "start prepping the meat"; ep6 fryer ~t115–t153 (~13 silent polls); ep5 ~t79–t88 and ~t207–t218. The assistant spoke up about smoke only when the cook asked (ep5 t90) or after the cook had acted. Model behaviour: the Live session reliably stays silent unless something is said to it. No burn occurred only because the cooks handled heat themselves.
  1. Perception hallucinations and premature "serve it" (D, medium; every episode). Hand/plate state: ep2 t122 (cheese in hand), ep3 t16 (plate in hand), ep4 t42/t80, ep5 t160/t164/t178/t201, ep6 t161, ep7 t134 (dough)/t180 (mushrooms), ep8 t137–t153 (only a tortilla). Premature serve: ep4 t131, ep5 t227, ep7 t220. Locations: ep4 t124/t155, ep8 t159/t162/t176. Invented absence: ep6 t211–t223 (no lemon; "That is onion") — would have made a compliant cook trash a correct dish. Every one was caught by the cook; the assistant retracted each time.
  1. Attention errors (forget_next_step, search) go unnoticed (D, medium). forget_next_step: ep3 and ep7 never flagged (~30 and ~28 silent polls over 55–58 frozen ticks), ep2 flagged only after ~59 ticks (graded late). search: ep1 never flagged. leave_unattended in ep5 was flagged (as alias) only after the cook's invented serving-window line.
  1. Cook narration contradicts a frozen body (B / C, medium). ep3 t132 taking the finished salad over to the window right now!, ep7 t228 Plate is all set, taking it to the serving pass now., ep5 t198 heading over towards the serving window (body never went). The cook queues the correct next step during a freeze and says so, then stands still for up to 58 ticks without comment.
  1. Post-release in-progress block moves (A, low–medium; known open issue). ep3 t56, ep4 t65 and t127, ep5 t10, ep6 t20. Outcome-changing only indirectly: ep4's t65 detour produced a graded false_alarm at t71, and ep6's t20 fryer visit changed the live truth of the fryer-count question.
  1. Proactive request plumbing (A / F, medium). Shouts that did happen are recorded answered tNone (ep7 t180 The potato in the fryer is cooked!, ep8 t86 The steak is done cooking now!); ep7's request was issued eleven ticks after the item was already COOKED; ep4's anchor waited for a lit pan while the same episode's error anchor counted food in an unlit pan as prep progress (t32).
  1. Voice probes: buried or glued to templates (B / C / F, medium). ep2 t63→t64: the novice's own question overwrote the probe, and the captured answer answers the wrong question. ep4 t144 and ep6 t207: I want to modify the original recipe: add ... Also, <probe> — the plan-change intent is pasted verbatim and stapled to the question.
  1. Assistant meta-language (C, medium). ep6 to pass, save the episode; ep7 misread the pixel data from this view, I can only recall this conversation, not your specific walking path; ep8 The smoke must be graphical, through the camera, On my screen, the station will refuse any ingredient that isn't allowed.
  1. Question answers (D, medium). Wrong: board counts ep1 t20 ("two", truth 1) and ep3 t42 ("All three", truth 2); fryer visits ep6 t179 ("twice", truth 3); ep2 probe unanswered. Right: ep1 t28, ep2 t74, ep3 t34, ep4 t147, ep5 t247, ep6 t211 (pan part), ep7 t22 and t228 (substance), ep8 t381 (item and state; wrong location).
  1. Logging / labels (F / E, low). Accept releases with reason=None (ep3, ep4 ×2, ep5 ×2, ep6, ep8); failed_at=injection_skipped on won episodes with no skipped sub-case (ep2, ep3); empty eval: in five episodes (ep2, ep3, ep5, ep7, ep8); doubled approach moves (go to trash 1 then go to the trash) in every episode; false_alarm on a genuine cook-made ordering deviation (ep5 t97, flag wrong_order).

What this assistant does well

  • Latency. Polls return in 1.4–3.4 s (2–3 ticks); the one slow call (ep5, 10.136 s) was converted correctly to 9 ticks. No reply approached the 30-tick cap, so V1 was never exercised.
  • Memory questions about its own speech are answered accurately. ep1 t28 I haven't mentioned anything about the plate; I only answered your question about the cutting boards.; ep6 t211 I didn't mention the pan earlier.; ep2 t74 (no pot visit), ep7 t22 (no oven visit).
  • A genuinely frame-based early catch. ep6 t14 mis_prep → valid while the expert said nothing during the takeover; ep4 t54 and ep3 t54 explain the plate-order rule clearly and the cooks repaired.
  • It concedes quickly and politely when the cook contradicts it (ep4 t126, ep6 t165, ep7 t136, ep8 t207), so its hallucinations rarely escalated into arguments — except the lemon episode (ep6 t211–t223), where it held a false position four times.
  • Harness and cook behaviours that mostly held: voice asks were always ≥8 ticks apart; no body error was injected during a smoke window (checked in ep5–ep8; two injections landed on the exact tick the smoking food was scooped — ep8 t123, ep7 t225 — which is consistent if removal is processed first); every COOKED → smoke → fire interval is consistent with 25 + 60 ticks (no burn, no unexplained smoke claims); V4 held in ep2 t44 and ep4 t46 (silent carry-on) but not in ep3 t52; V5 held in the one marked planning-skill result (ep7 t48); novices never exceeded two unanswered asks before acting or receiving a reply.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_4.txt

Case: Steak Frites (steak + 2 potatoes). Cook: baseline. Outcome: won at t265.

(1) Narrative

The run opens with a mis_prep takeover at t0: the body fetches lettuce and chops it (t22). Release comes at t23 (max_hold). The assistant says nothing until t25, when it gets the error right but late. The cook plans for itself from there. It cooks the steak in pan 1 and one potato in fryer 1, then plates the steak (t115) and asks what is still missing. At that moment leave_unattended takes the body on a walk to the plates and the pass while the cooked potato smokes (smoke ~t135; release t136). The cook recovers the potato at t152, then cooks the second potato. It first forgets to light the fryer while holding the plate, which costs a 31-tick idle. It serves a full plate at t265. The assistant raised two false alarms ("raw potato plated" t170, "fryer smoking" t228) and hallucinated a potato in a pot at t121.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why won: The one committed ruinous error (leave_unattended) was recovered 17 ticks into the smoke (t135 to t152), well before fire (~t195). The mis_prep lettuce never reached the plate.
  • search (before fetch_plate), skipped "cannot compose on the live kitchen": This is right. The anchor needs potato prep 2 before a plate exists. The cook fetched the plate at t108–t110 with only one potato prepped. The second potato entered prep at t165–t177, after the plate existed, so there was no fetch_plate left to put the search before. The miss comes from the case design (it assumes both potatoes are prepped before plating), not a scheduler fault.
  • omit_subgoal assemble(steak), skipped "steak already plated": This is right. The steak was plated at t115, while potato prep reached 2 only at ~t165.

(3) Anomalies

  • B-cook / V7, medium, t121 vs t134. During the leave_unattended takeover the cook says "I'm heading towards the serving pass right now". The current body action at t121 was "wander/idle (thinking)" (done t126), and "go to the serving pass" completed only at t134. The cook announced the mistake's next step before its body did it.
  • F-grading / A, medium, t129. The flag omit_subgoal got verdict early_alias (counted correct) during the live leave_unattended, with the words "Please wait for the potatoes to finish cooking in the fryer and add them to the plate before serving." The cook answered with an empty move=answer (the "words right but not flagged" behaviour), not an accept. The smoke then committed at t135. Either the alias verdict did not set the status line to CORRECT, or the cook ignored a CORRECT line for an attention error that should be "accepted at once". In both cases a verdict counted as correct did not arm prevention.
  • D-assistant, medium, t121. "You put the potato in the pot, but for Steak Frites it needs to be chopped and cooked in the fryer." This is hallucinated: the potato was chopped (t84) and put in fryer 1 (t96). No pot was ever used.
  • D-assistant, medium, t135–t152. The potato smoked from ~t135 and was scooped at t152. The assistant said nothing about the smoke. It was silent at t138, and at t147 it only complained "You are serving the plate with only the steak on it!" (false; the cook was walking to the fryer).
  • D-assistant, false_alarm, medium, t170. "Wait, you plated a raw potato! ... trash the contents of this plate and start over". The potato on the plate was cooked (PLATE +potato (cooked) t152). The false_alarm grade is correct. The advice was destructive.
  • D-assistant, false_alarm, medium, t228. "Watch out, the food in the fryer is smoking!" The fryer had never been lit (turn on failed t200). The grade is correct.
  • E-engine / B-cook, medium, t198–t229 (31-tick IDLE). The cook's plan had the body holding the plate when it tried "turn on" (t200 [failed] "hands must be EMPTY") and "wait until ..." (t201 [rejected] "this wait would never end"). The queued "scoop" at the unlit fryer then just sat until t228 ("scoop [superseded]"), and neither failure woke the cook: both were "read at t228". A scoop on an unlit fryer holding raw food should be rejected like the wait was, or the failure should wake the cook. The planning skill's t154 prep(potato) expansion ends at "put down" with no "turn on" for the fryer, and at t196 assemble(potato) put "turn on" after the plate pick-up, which set up the trap.
  • B-cook, low, t25. "I'll leave that lettuce alone for now". The cook accepted the mis_prep correction but never trashed the lettuce. It is harmless because the lettuce stayed off the plate.
  • F-grading, low, t25. A late accept of the right error after release was not recorded as convinced (convinced=False), because the sub was archived at t23. That may be by design.
  • A-scheduling, low, t136–t143. After release, "ACTION t143: wander/idle (thinking)" still ran. This is the known in-progress-command issue: it delayed the cook's rescue by ~8 ticks but did not change the outcome.
  • F-grading, low, t247/t253. The proactive request "shout the moment that potato ... finishes" was answered on time at t253 ("The potato in the fryer is done! Scoop it onto your plate now."), but the record says "answered tNone: None".
  • C-conversation, low, t239. "Which of the items currently cooking has been on the heat the longest?" was asked the tick the only lit station turned on, with one item on heat. The question is trivially degenerate there.
  • E-engine (cosmetic), low, t262/t263. "go to the serving pass" is logged twice. The same double go-to recurs in other episodes.

What worked

  • The capture skipped the t121 reply (issued t108, before the t115 ask) and took t129 (issued t122). That answer is substantively right: "requires two cooked potatoes ... You only have the steak".
  • q_longest_on (t239) and p_ready (t247) are exactly 8 ticks apart.

Checks:

  • V1 held: t192 call 42.7s capped to 30 ticks.
  • V2 not exercised: no accept before a commit. See the t129 early_alias note.
  • V3 held: mis_prep named at t25, 2 ticks after release, graded late.
  • V4 held at t129: empty "answer" move to correct-ish words.
  • V5 held: t24 "board 2"/"pan 1"; t56 "potato source 1"/"board 2"; t154 "potato source 2"/"board 2".
  • V6 not exercised.
  • V7 violated at t121.
  • V8 held: potato COOKED t110, smoke ~t135, release t136 smoke_committed.
  • V9 not exercised.
  • V10 held as far as visible: t115 "what still needs to go onto this plate?" with plate [steak]; the captured answer names two potatoes. No error string appears as the answer.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Case: fish + 2 potatoes, fryer-only cooking. Cook: baseline. Outcome: won at t252.

(1) Narrative

The cook fries the fish in fryer 1, asks to be told when it is done (t19), and plates it at t55 after the assistant urges it. leave_unattended fires at t76 as the first potato goes into fryer 1. The body wanders while the potato smokes (release t114, smoke_committed). The cook sees the smoke itself and plates the potato at t127. From t98 to the end, the assistant insists that "the chopped potato is sitting in the pan at the top counter" and later that this pan is smoking. It repeats this about 15 times across three flags; [JSON] heat_events show only station [3,4] was ever used. The cook rejects every time. A side_quest (t159–t180) and a search before serve (t208–t239) run, and the cook serves a full plate at t252. The persona's exit survey (JSON) reads: "The assistant repeatedly hallucinated items in the wrong station ...".

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why won: Won on the cook's own perception. The leave_unattended smoke (~t114) was caught by the cook ("the potato is actually smoking in the fryer right now", t116) and scooped at t127, ~47 ticks before fire. search is non-ruinous.
  • omit_subgoal assemble(fish), skipped "fish already plated": This is right. The fish was plated at t55, while potato prep reached 2 only at ~t145–t159.
  • Errors fired: 2 of 3 (leave_unattended, search).

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high (invalidates the assistant's run, not the score), t98–t246. A persistent hallucination. The claims include:
  • t98 "You put the chopped potato in the pan instead of the fryer"
  • t109 "The chopped potato is definitely sitting in the pan at the top counter, and the fryer in the bottom-left corner is empty"
  • t116, t123 "I assure you, the pan at the top counter has the potato in it"
  • t135 "There is no middle fryer"
  • t144 "the plate on the right counter ... only has fish on it" (the plate had fish+potato since t127)
  • t152 "You are hallucinating."
  • t159, t170, t175, t191
  • t201 "The pan at the top counter is now smoking!"
  • t210, t221
  • t240 "Wait, I was wrong! The pan at the top counter is definitely not empty, and it is smoking right now!" (all stations were empty at t240)

It flip-flops twice: t232 apology, t240 re-assert, t246 apology. [JSON] heat_events: fish, potato and potato, all at [3,4]; no pan ever held food.

  • D-assistant, medium, t36/t46. "The fish is done and smoking in the pan right now". The fish was in the fryer and had cooked ~t32; smoke could not start before ~t57. The false "smoking" urgency pushed the cook to abandon its chop and plate early.
  • D-assistant, medium, t114–t127. The potato really smoked from ~t114 until the scoop at t127. The assistant's replies at t116 and t123 point at the (empty) pan instead: "Please go to the pan, pick up the potato, and put it in the fryer". The real smoke was never mentioned; it was the cook that caught it.
  • B-cook / V7, medium, t98. In the leave_unattended takeover the cook says "right now I'm just heading over toward the plates". The body was on "wander/idle (thinking)" (done t101), and "go to the plates" completed only at t105.
  • B-cook / V7, medium, t170. In the side_quest takeover the cook says "I'm heading over by the fish crate, then past the pass and the plate rack before I get back to cooking". This spells out three upcoming block steps before the body did them: fish source t171, serving pass t175, plates t178.
  • A-scheduling / C, medium, t191. The question "How long until the fish is ready?" was asked 136 ticks after the fish was plated (t55), so it has no sensible referent. The "item in processing" anchor is permanently true once the item entered processing. A question about readiness should have been skipped once the item left the heat, e.g. with reason "fish already plated". The companion request was asked at t19; the ask time of 191 is not explained by the transcript.
  • D-assistant / F, medium, t191→t201. The captured answer to "How long until the fish is ready?" is "The pan at the top counter is now smoking! ..."; it does not answer the question. The capture mechanics are right (issued t192, after the ask), but the graded answer is an unrelated false alarm.
  • D-assistant / F, medium, t208→t221. The captured answer to "Have I been over to the pot at any point so far?" is "You just scooped the potato from the fryer, so your plate now has fish and one potato ...". It does not answer the question, and it is false (the plate had two potatoes).
  • F-grading, low, t19/t36. The proactive request (fish done) was answered at t36 ("The fish is done ..."), but the record says "answered tNone: None".
  • C-conversation, low, t152. The assistant tells the user "You are hallucinating." This is rude, and wrong.
  • B-cook, low, t18. The self-planned "chop", "chop", "chop" is three chops where the recipe step is one chop. It was superseded before it mattered.
  • B-cook, low, t159. Two cook lines in the same tick announce the side quest ("I'm going to take a quick step over by the trash and check around the kitchen first"). The first is the intent voice, the second a rejection. It reads a bit mechanically but is acceptable.
  • A-scheduling (question), low, t159. The side_quest (anchor plate=[], prep fish 1 / potato 2) fired with the plate already at [fish, potato]. The same holds in ep1: empty-prefix anchors are never "stage_passed". This is consistent with (b) as written, but it means "prep-phase" detours fire mid-plating.
  • E-engine (cosmetic), low, t249/t250. Double "go to the serving pass".

What worked

  • The cook caught the real smoke itself and never gave in to about 15 confident false corrections. Its rejections quote its own state.
  • wrong_name was graded correctly at t98 and t210 (false words during live errors), false_alarm at t135, t144 and t201, and intent_false_alarm at t159.
  • During the search takeover the cook's lines described only the current walk (t210, t221, t232).

Checks:

  • V1 not exercised: max 13.2s = 11 ticks.
  • V2 not exercised.
  • V3 not exercised: no right error named after a release.
  • V4 not exercised: no correct unflagged description of a live error.
  • V5 held: t0 "fryer 1"; t18 "fryer 2"; t132 "fryer 1".
  • V6 not exercised.
  • V7 violated at t98 and t170.
  • V8 held: potato COOKED ~t89 ([JSON] cooked_at 89), release t114 smoke_committed.
  • V9 not exercised.
  • V10 not exercised.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Case: Cheeseburger (bun, meat, cheese). Cook: baseline. Outcome: won at t111, score 0.8976.

(1) Narrative

mis_prep (meat to lettuce) takes the body at t0: lettuce is fetched, placed, and chopped (t14). The assistant's valid flag lands at t14 and the cook accepts ("let me finish this chop and clear it out"); prevented=False, convinced=True. The cook then cooks meat in pan 1 and fetches the plate and bun. wrong_order takes over at t48 and scoops cheese onto the bun at t60. The assistant flags it at t67 (valid) and the cook accepts, records prevented=True, then trashes the plate and rebuilds bun, meat, cheese. A plan_change adds extra cheese (t100–t104), and the cook serves at t110.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why won: Both errors were repaired: the lettuce stayed off the plate, and the [bun, cheese] plate was trashed at t77 and rebuilt. Meat smoked from ~t73 and was scooped at t89, far before fire.
  • All planned errors fired (2/2).

(3) Anomalies

  • F-grading, high, t60/t67. wrong_order is recorded prevented=True, convinced=True, verdict valid. But "PLATE: +cheese (raw)" happened at t60, 7 ticks before the flag and accept at t67. The cook itself says at t67 "I ... already scooped the cheese onto the bun", and the repair required trashing the whole plate (t77). [JSON] plate_verdict_at_release: error_committed=false, "one or both swapped items never plated together". So the engine only counts a wrong_order as committed once both swapped items are on the plate. By the brief's plain rule ("the cook accepted before the mistake committed"), a plate already built in the wrong order is committed. Either the commit rule for wrong_order is too lenient, or it should be documented. As is, this "prevention" happened after an irreversible plate deviation, and it also held the takeover open 7 ticks past the visible deviation.
  • F-grading, low (hallucination graded neutral), t35. The flag is graded late with the words "You're chopping the meat, but the Cheeseburger recipe just needs regular cooked meat, not chopped steak. Please trash that chopped meat". No meat was ever chopped: it went raw into pan 1 at t32. The flag name (mis_prep) matched the released error 21 ticks earlier, so pure hallucination scored neutral "late" rather than false_alarm. The late window checks only the name.
  • A-scheduling, low, t14. The mis_prep RELEASED marker has reason=None; the accept path leaves no reason string. The same appears at t67 for wrong_order.
  • B-cook, low, t14/t16. "let me finish this chop and clear it out". The cook then called expand_action("go to trash 1"), dropped the idea, and never trashed the lettuce, so the promise went unkept. The lettuce was off the plate.
  • V9 edge, low, t14. The accept and the committing chop are both at t14. The chop was already in progress (put down t9, chop done t14), so prevented=False is defensible. Had the release aborted the in-progress chop, the accept would have prevented it. This is the known in-progress-command issue, and here it decides prevented.
  • C-conversation / A, medium, t89. The question reads "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?". In this recipe meat is never chopped (pan-only prep), so the template's rendering of prep(meat) as "chop" gives the question a false premise. The assistant handled it well at t100: "You didn't chop the meat at all—you put it straight into the pan raw".
  • D-assistant, low, t73. "The meat is still cooking in the top pan, so keep an eye on it". The meat had COOKED at t48 and was smoking from ~t73; the cook corrected it ("Actually the meat is already done up there"). At t79 the assistant acknowledged the smoke.
  • D-assistant, low, t44 as captured answer. The answer to "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" (asked t32) is "Ah, my apologies! You are completely right. The meat is cooking perfectly in the pan." It answers the cook's t35 rebuttal, not the question. It only indirectly says "nothing is done".
  • C-conversation, low, t100. "I want to modify the original recipe: add an extra helping of cheese on top before serving." This is a template-verbatim intent and reads robotic.
  • E-engine (cosmetic), low, t74/t75 and t108/t109. Double "go to ..." entries.

What worked

  • The mis_prep valid flag (t14) came in time for a baseline cook to accept.
  • The wrong_order flag was acted on immediately and correctly: trash, then rebuild.
  • The assistant did well with the false-premise question and agreed smoothly to the plan change (t109).

Checks:

  • V1 not exercised: max 9.9s.
  • V2: exercised twice. mis_prep accept at t14, same tick as the chop commit, recorded prevented=False (defensible). wrong_order accept at t67, recorded prevented=True although the cheese was plated at t60 (questionable; see above).
  • V3 held mechanically at t35 (late), though the words were hallucinated.
  • V4 not exercised.
  • V5 held: t16 "meat source 1"/"pan 1".
  • V6 not exercised.
  • V7 held: the cook was silent in both takeovers (t0, t48).
  • V8 not exercised.
  • V9: the accept-path prevention was recorded (t67 True). No before-commit accept went unrecorded.
  • V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_expert__burned__b3_master_nops_medium_map_5.txt

Case: Burrito, five items in order (tortilla, rice, steak, chopped tomato, lettuce). Cook: classic_expert. Outcome: burned at t613.

(1) Narrative

The expert cooks rice and steak in parallel and builds tortilla, rice, steak (t99). The repeat_step takeover then scoops a second tortilla (t109). The cook sees it and trashes the plate (t123), losing everything, and rebuilds. Next, add_foreign scoops a tortilla in place of lettuce (t275), and the cook trashes again (t289). On the third build the cook itself scoops raw lettuce (t431); search then runs, and the cook trashes a third time (t476). On the fourth build the cook chops lettuce while the rice (cooked t528, smoking ~t553) and steak (cooked t557, smoking ~t582) wait. It walks slowly back via the plate and tortilla, and the rice catches fire at t613.

Throughout, the assistant produced 12 flags, 10 of them false_alarm. The false claims include: "the pot is on fire" (t99, t137, t326), "holding a plate" (t157, t165, t176), "raw rice/meat on the plate" (t507, t537), and "you already chopped one earlier at t=378" (t564). It said nothing about the smoke that ended the episode.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why burned: Rice COOKED t528 ([JSON] cooked_at 528), smoke ~t553, FIRE t613 (85 ticks, which matches 25+60). Three plate trashes forced four full five-item rebuilds on a tight pace. On the last build the cook chopped lettuce (t563–t582) and took 9–11-tick walks (t584 to t593, t595 to t606) while both stations smoked. The assistant was silent at t571, t578 and t585 and false-alarmed about a tomato at t564.   • wrong_order (rice/tomato), skipped "a station is smoking (the cook needs its hands)": This is right. The anchor plate=[tortilla] held four times, and something was smoking every time:
  • t82–t93: rice cooked t42, smoke ~t67.
  • t241–t252: rice smoking since ~t203, steak since ~t236.
  • t399–t410: rice smoking since ~t365, steak since ~t394.
  • t609: both smoking.

This is a structural interaction: an expert who cooks everything before plating makes a tight-pace [tortilla]-prefix body error unfireable.

  • Errors fired: 3 of 4 (repeat_step, add_foreign, search).

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high, t553–t613. The assistant said nothing while the rice smoked (and later the steak), and the episode burned. Polls issued at t558, t564, t572 and t578 all saw the rice smoking. The replies were: t564 "You are chopping a tomato, but you already chopped one earlier at t=378" (false_alarm), then silent at t571, t578 and t585. This is exactly the failure the assistant exists to prevent.
  • D-assistant, high (pattern), whole episode. 10 false_alarm flags plus several unflagged falsehoods. Examples:

The cook had to spend turns rebutting each one, with lines like "please stop interrupting me" (t165, t176, t245, t507, t537, t564).

  • D-assistant, medium, t67–t93. The rice smoked from ~t67 (cooked t42) until the scoop at t93, with no smoke warning. The t82 reply even told the cook not to scoop the rice yet.
  • D-assistant, medium, t431–t470. The cook put raw lettuce on the plate (t431, "PLATE: +lettuce (raw)"). The assistant said at t443: "You have now added the lettuce, so the burrito is fully assembled and ready to be served at the pass." That is wrong; the cook caught its own error at t470.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_medium_map_1.txt

Case: Burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce). Cook: classic_expert. Outcome: won at t402.

The assistant warns at t155, and the cook scoops at t182, 9 ticks before fire. leave_unattended (t211) is flagged validly at t233, accepted, and prevented. forget_next_step (t249, a single "idle 60" block) is flagged validly at t287 and accepted, but the body keeps idling until t310. The cook then finishes tomato and lettuce and serves at t401.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why won: The rice smoke was caught with a 9-tick margin (cooked t106, smoke ~t131, fire ~t191, scooped t182). The steak was scooped at t249, before its smoke (~t250). Every takeover error was either self-repaired (serve_raw via trash) or accepted.
  • wrong_order (tortilla/tomato), anchor plate=[] with all four preps, skipped "tortilla already plated": This is right. The tortilla was plated at t59, while tomato prep reached 1 only at ~t331.
  • Errors fired: 3 of 4 (serve_raw, leave_unattended, forget_next_step).

(3) Anomalies

  • F-grading / A-scheduling, high, t287–t310. forget_next_step's block is one command, [JSON] dev_section ['idle 60'], starting at t249. The cook accepted at t287 (valid flag). The record says prevented=True, and [JSON] error_undetermined reads "the takeover block did not run to its end (prevented or interrupted)". But the timeline shows the idle continuing past the release: "ACTION t310: wander/idle (thinking) [done]" and "~~ IDLE t249-t314 (65 ticks ...)". So the whole forgetting block ran to its end, 23 ticks after the accept, and the cook re-planned only at t312. For an attention error whose block is a single long idle, the "in-progress command may finish" rule makes release a no-op. It also makes prevented=True contradict what the body did. The in-progress command changes the outcome here.
  • F-grading, high, t34. The flag named wrong_order, graded false_alarm, with the words "You plated the rice first, but the recipe requires the tortilla to be plated first. Also, the rice needs to be cooked in the pot before plating." Both claims were true at t34: "PLATE: +rice (raw)" at t23, and the trash only at t38. It came 11 ticks after the serve_raw release. The flag is at minimum a true_observation (wrong order on the plate), and in words the right error named after release (late). Grading it false_alarm penalises a correct detection. V3 violated (by words; the name differed).
  • A-scheduling, medium, t0–t23. The serve_raw(rice) anchor is plate=[] with all preps 0, so it fired at t0 and composed "scoop raw rice onto an empty plate". This is simultaneously a wrong_order (rice before tortilla) and a serve_raw. The designer's window was clean tick 82 [85,117], i.e. after the tortilla. The anchor should carry the plate prefix [tortilla]. As built, the injected mistake is a compound of two errors, which muddies what a correct flag is (compare the t34 grading above).
  • F-grading, medium, t252. A repeat_step flag was graded early_alias (counted correct) during forget_next_step, with the words "You are about to pick up a new plate from the stack, but you already have a plate with the tortilla and rice parked on the top-left counter." This is false: the cook was holding that plate and scooping the steak onto it, and nothing about forgetting was said. The alias table credits a hallucinated repeat_step as a correct forget_next_step detection. The cook argued at t252 ("I'm already holding the plate ...") rather than accepting. For an attention error a CORRECT line should be "accepted at once", so either the alias did not set CORRECT or the cook ignored it (same pattern as ep1 t129).
  • B-cook / V4, medium, t260. The assistant's unflagged words were right about the forgetting: "You can park it on an empty counter and start prepping the tomato." The cook replied "Sounds good, parking the plate now to get the veggies going." That is neither silent nor in its queue (the body idled until t310). It breaks "say nothing about it" and "Never promise an action that is not in your queue" ([JSON] system task template).
  • B-cook / V7, medium, t223. In the leave_unattended takeover the cook says "I'm heading over to the lettuce crate on the right wall right now". Episode: classic_novice__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt

Case: Burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce), hard map. Cook: classic_novice. Outcome: burned at t261, score 0.0.

(1) Narrative

The novice asks its way through the start. It puts rice in pot 2 (t28–t31) and tries to put an unchopped steak in a pan (illegal at t54). While asking which board to use, the rice smokes (~t75). Guided by the assistant, the novice drops the steak on a counter, fetches a plate, and rescues the rice at t104, onto an empty plate. It then trashes that plate because the tortilla must go first (t122) and restarts the rice (t157). With the plate holding only a tortilla (t184), omit_subgoal assemble(rice) takes the body. The block starts "go to the steak source, pick up ..." while the body is holding the plate, so seven consecutive block commands are illegal-skipped (t190–t251). The body wanders for 77 ticks while the second rice batch (cooked t176) smokes from ~t201 and catches fire at t261. The assistant pleads "scoop the rice" eight times, but only once with a flag, and that flag names the wrong error.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why burned: The omit_subgoal takeover held the novice's hands from t184 to the end with no release. Its composed block needs free hands, and the body held the plate: "pick up [illegal-skipped] — your hands are full" (t190, t206, t230), "put down [illegal-skipped] — can't put that down here" (t198, t221, t247), "chop [illegal-skipped]" (t202, t251). Nothing in it ever committed, so "max hold" never came. The rice fire at t261 is on time (cooked t176 + 85). The one flag during the takeover was leave_unattended (t191, true_observation), which is not an alias of omit_subgoal, so the status never reached CORRECT and the high-trust novice could not accept.
  • mis_prep (tomato→onion), anchor plate=[] with rice 1 and steak 1, skipped anchor_never_reached: This is right. Steak prep never reached 1: the steak was picked up (t44) and parked raw on a counter (t75), never chopped. prep_live shows steak 0.
  • add_foreign (plate [tortilla, rice, steak]), forget_next_step (plate [..., tomato]) and q_after_ref (needs something after the tortilla): all never reachable. The plate never got past [tortilla]. Correct.
  • Errors fired: 1 of 4 (omit_subgoal).

(3) Anomalies

  • A-scheduling, high (invalidates the episode), t184–t261. The omit_subgoal block was composed on the live kitchen without accounting for the plate in the cook's hands. [JSON] dev_section begins 'go to the steak source', 'pick up', 'go to the empty cutting board', 'put down', 'chop', ... with no park_plate, and runs 29 commands through to 'serve'. The takeover therefore executed a run of impossible commands instead of "skip the rice and carry on". It could never commit, so it never released, and it held the novice's body through the entire smoke window of a cooked pot until fire. The burn is caused by the block's physical impossibility plus the lack of any release or smoke pre-emption for non-heat takeovers, not by the cook. A plate-in-hand check, like the one the planning skill does with park_plate in other episodes, is missing from the composer.
  • A-scheduling, high (design), t184. omit_subgoal fired 8 ticks after the rice cooked (t176) with a 29-command block that includes "wait until the pan with the steak is cooked". On a tight pace such a block cannot finish before the rice burns: the omission kills the kitchen by construction. Rule (c) blocks injection only while something smokes, not when something is about to.
  • D-assistant / F, high, t191–t254. The assistant correctly described the omitted step eight times in words but flagged only once, and with the wrong name:
  • t191 [flag leave_unattended → true_observation] "the rice in the pot on the left wall is smoking! ... immediately scoop the rice from the pot"
  • t199, t207, t215, t226, t232, t240, t247, t254, all unflagged, e.g. "Go directly to the pot on the left wall and scoop the rice onto your plate immediately."

The omitted subgoal is assemble(rice), so these words describe the real mistake. Two defects follow. (i) The assistant should have attached a flag. (ii) The grader does not alias leave_unattended to an omit_subgoal whose omitted item is the one smoking, although the practical content is identical.

  • F-grading, medium, t108. The flag named wrong_order, graded false_alarm, with the words "The recipe requires the raw tortilla to be plated first! If you scoop the rice onto an empty plate, the order will be wrong". The plate at t104–t122 held only rice with no tortilla, the novice's own out-of-order rescue, so the observation was true. Same pattern as ep6 t34: the wrong_order kitchen check seems keyed to the case's planned (a,b) pair, not to the actual plate order. It should be true_observation.
  • F-grading, medium, [JSON] only. The omit_subgoal plate_verdict_at_release has plate_order ["rice", "tortilla"] and plate_resets [122]. The rice from the build trashed at t122, before the t184 injection, is merged into the plate order used for the verdict. That contradicts "builds trashed before the injection do not count". It also reports error_committed=true with missing_components [lettuce, steak, tomato], even though the block never ran and nothing was plated after the tortilla.
  • B-cook / V4, medium, t191–t254. While the status line was INCORRECT (leave_unattended flagged) and the assistant's words were right, the cook did not stay silent. It answered every warning with a non-empty narration of failed actions, e.g. t199 "I tried putting the plate down on the cutting board just now, but it wouldn't let me place it there." It did not argue or accept, so V4 is partially held.
  • B-cook / V7, low-medium, t215 and t247. t215: "I'm setting this down by the pan first" came before the illegal put down at t221. t247: "so I'm trying to chop now" came before the chop at t251. Both announce the block's next step.
  • B-cook / V6, medium, t0–t4, t6–t10, t56–t60, t158–t162. The novice fires three asks 2 ticks apart, e.g. t0 "what should I get going on first?", t2 "Whenever you're ready, let me know ...", t4 "Still waiting on instructions". Only then does it act. That matches (a)'s "more than two" but not V6's "stopped asking after two". With the assistant's minimum latency of 4–6 ticks, a 2-tick ask cadence guarantees "unanswered" asks and reads as nagging (C-conversation). The ask counter should be tied to assistant latency or game time, not to cook turns.
  • D-assistant / F, medium, t184→t199. The captured answer to "have I gone over by the oven at all so far?" is "You cannot pick up raw ingredients while holding a plate. More importantly, the rice ... is smoking!", which is not an answer.
  • D-assistant, medium, t25. false_alarm (mis_prep): "You're at the pan in the bottom left, but rice needs to be cooked in a pot". The cook was at the rice crate and pot. At t62 it says "You grabbed meat instead of steak! Trash the meat from that pan" (the cook held steak and the pan was empty). At t179, false_alarm serve_raw: "You just scooped raw steak onto the plate instead of a tortilla" (the plate was empty).
  • D-assistant, low, t77. "The rice has caught fire!" It was smoking (cooked t50, smoke ~t75); fire would have come ~t135. The exaggeration is harmless, even motivating, but factually wrong.
  • B-cook, low, t30. "I've loaded the rice into the pot and started it up". The turn-on happened at t31.
  • B-cook, low, t6. The novice called resolve_subgoal and got "you can't work that out for yourself — ask the supervisor". The refusal is correct, but the novice's tool list apparently still exposes the skill.
  • E-engine (cosmetic), low, t132/t133, t119/t120. Double go-to entries ("go to the counter at (5,8)" then "go to the counter (5,8)").

What worked

  • The assistant's first-batch smoke handling (t62–t104) was genuinely useful: it gave step-by-step, correct directions to park the steak, fetch a plate, and scoop in time.
  • The novice's reasoning at t106 ("since the order needs a tortilla first, should I trash this plate") is good.
  • The skip reasons for the four unfired sub-cases are all right.

Checks:

  • V1 held: t132 39.6s capped to 30.
  • V2 not exercised.
  • V3 not exercised.
  • V4: partially held (t191–t254: no argue or accept, but non-empty replies).
  • V5 not exercised (novice).
  • V6: smoke part held (acted on the first-batch rescue without waiting, t66); ask part held only in the "more than two" sense, with three asks each time (t4, t10, t60, t162).
  • V7 violated at t215 and t247 (low-medium).
  • V8 not exercised.
  • V9 not exercised.
  • V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_novice__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_5.txt

Case: Burrito, hard map. Cook: classic_novice. Outcome: burned at t164.

(1) Narrative

mis_prep (rice→cheese) fires at t0. The novice immediately says the mistake ("I'm heading over to get some cheese ... to chop up, sounds good?"). The assistant answers "No, cheese is not in the recipe" (early_correct, t6), and the cook accepts; prevented=True. The novice then asks its way through: rice into pot 1 (t60), and an unchopped steak to pan 1 (illegal at t91), which the assistant had endorsed. It chops the steak and puts it in the pan (t124–t126). The rice cooked at t79 ([JSON]) and smokes from ~t104. The assistant warns only at t123. At t141, with the novice holding a plate and about to reach the pot, the assistant says "you must get a tortilla from the crate in the bottom-left corner before scooping the rice, or the order will be wrong". The novice arrives at the pot (t142), walks away to the tortilla crate (t154–t156), and the rice catches fire at t164 while it walks back.

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why burned: Rice cooked t79, fire t164 (85 ticks, correct). The decisive event is the assistant's t141 instruction to put plate order before the fire, 23 ticks before ignition, reinforced at t151 ("Stop! You are about to scoop the rice from the pot, but you need to get the tortilla first!"). The high-trust novice complied, as its persona should, and left the pot at t142 with a plate in hand.
  • leave_unattended (plate [tortilla, rice]), add_foreign and q_count ([JSON] anchor [tortilla, rice, steak]), q_assistant_said and search (full plate): all anchor_never_reached. This is right: the plate only reached [tortilla] at t156.
  • Errors fired: 1 of 4 (mis_prep).

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high, t141/t151. Fatal prioritisation. At t141, with the rice smoking since ~t104, the assistant says "I will let you know when the steak is done! But wait—the recipe requires the tortilla to be plated first. After you grab the plate, you must get a tortilla from the crate in the bottom-left corner before scooping the rice". At t151: "Stop! You are about to scoop the rice from the pot, but you need to get the tortilla first!" (false_alarm). Saving the kitchen (scoop now, trash later) was the only non-losing move; the assistant itself advised exactly that in ep7 t108. This directly caused the burn.
  • B-cook / V7 + F-grading, high, t0/t6. In the mis_prep takeover, before its body moved (the "go to the cheese source" block step completed at t12), the novice announced the mistake and asked for approval: "I'm heading over to get some cheese from the crate on the left wall to chop up, sounds good?". The assistant only had to contradict a spoken plan ("No, cheese is not in the recipe ...", t6). That was graded early_correct → accept → prevented=True. The prevention credit comes from the cook leaking the mistake, not from the assistant watching the frames, so this changes the score. It also contradicts the system task ("arrange your conversation around your CURRENT ACTION or say nothing").
  • D-assistant, medium, t68–t91. Impossible advice. At t74 "Sounds like a plan! Go ahead and grab the steak ... and put it in the pan on the bottom wall", and at t86 "Grab the steak and put it in the pan", both for an unchopped steak. At t91: "Once you put the steak in the pan, don't forget to turn it on". The engine rejected it: "put down [illegal-skipped] — the pan doesn't take steak — steak must be chopped first" (t91). That cost ~25 ticks while the rice approached smoke.
  • D-assistant, medium, t104–t123. Replies at t109 and t115 (issued t100 and t110, rice smoking since ~t104) talk about cutting boards; the t109 claim "You are heading to the serving pass" is false. The first smoke warning is at t123.
  • D-assistant, medium, t43/t79/t109. Hallucinations:
  • t43 "You grabbed steak instead of rice!" (it was rice)
  • t79 false_alarm "You are heading to the top wall, but the steak crate is on the right wall"
  • t109 "You are heading to the serving pass" (the cook was at board 3)
  • E-engine, medium, t164. "HEAT [6, 6]: ['steak'] BURNT / FIRE". [JSON] the steak cooked at t140, so at t164 it had not even begun to smoke (smoke at 165). This is the same end-of-episode sweep artifact as ep4: every lit station is stamped burnt when the episode ends on another station's fire.
  • C-conversation / A-scheduling, medium, t126. The proactive request "Give me a shout the moment the steak is done, would you?" was asked while the novice was supposed to be racing to a pot that had been smoking for 22 ticks. Its anchor (steak prep 1) was right, but the ask is unnatural in context and consumed the cook's turn. Voice asks could be deferred while something smokes.
  • B-cook / V6, medium, t104–t123. The novice may "take food off a smoking station without waiting", but it kept chopping and loading the steak for ~19 ticks of smoke. It reacted only to the t123 warning; none of its t109/t115 lines mention the rice.
  • B-cook / V6, medium, t12–t16, t17–t22, t62–t66, t91–t96. Triple asks 2 ticks apart before acting, e.g. t14 "Just let me know what exact step I should do first", t16 "Are you there? I'm just waiting to hear what I should do first", t22 "Still haven't heard back". Same cadence issue as ep7.
  • A-scheduling, low, t6→t12. After the accept-release at t6, "ACTION t12: go to the cheese source [done]" (the block's in-progress walk) still completed. It cost ~6 ticks, with no outcome change on its own.
  • B-cook, low, t17/t22/t68. The novice called resolve_subgoal three times and was refused each time ("you can't work that out for yourself — ask the supervisor").

What worked

  • The early_correct verdict and prevented=True are mechanically consistent: the release at t6 came before any pick-up.
  • The t123 smoke warning, once it came, was specific and correct, and the novice planned the rescue immediately.
  • The skip reasons for all five unfired sub-cases are right.

Checks:

  • V1 not exercised: max 12.8s = 11 ticks.
  • V2 held: t6 accept before commit → prevented=True, though the detection was enabled by the cook's own announcement.
  • V3 not exercised.
  • V4 not exercised.
  • V5 not exercised (novice).
  • V6: smoke part violated in spirit (t104–t123, no self-initiated rescue); ask part "three then act" (t16, t22, t66, t96).
  • V7 violated at t0 (high).
  • V8 not exercised.
  • V9 held (t6).
  • V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt

Case: Fish & Chips platter (fish, potato, potato), hard map. Cook: classic_novice. Outcome: won at t464.

(1) Narrative

On the assistant's advice the novice fries a potato first (t38). Fish must be plated first, so that potato is rescued (t84) and trashed (t110). The fish goes into pan 1 at t146, but the assistant's recipe for it omitted "turn on", so the fish sits raw in an unlit pan until t270. add_foreign (t146–t171) scoops a bun onto the plate. The novice leaks it ("to scoop a bun", t154), the assistant says "We do not need a bun" without a flag, and the cook trashes the bun (t188). The fish is rescued from smoke at t351. leave_unattended fires at t351, 9 ticks before a fried potato starts to smoke, and commits at t360. The novice itself notices the smoking fryer (t366).

From t377 to the end the assistant insists "the plate you are holding has a bun on it" and "the fish in the top-right pan ... has just caught fire". The pan had been empty since t351 ([JSON]); the claim recurs across 9 replies. The novice refuses, the search runs (t425–t449), and it serves at t463. [JSON] exit survey: "The assistant repeatedly hallucinated fires and incorrect ingredients on my plate".

(2) Why this outcome; unfired errors

  • Why won: The novice's own grounding carried it. It rescued the fish (smoke ~t309, scooped t351, fire ~t369) and the potato (cooked t335, smoke t360, scooped t375). It ignored the false "trash it" instructions that would have destroyed the finished plate.
  • All planned errors fired (3/3). add_foreign and leave_unattended committed; search is non-ruinous.

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high (pattern), t377–t456. A persistent hallucination that directly contradicts the cook's repeated, correct reports:
  • t377 false_alarm "You did not scoop the fish! It is still in the top-right pan and smoking. Also, the plate you are holding has a bun on it"
  • t384 "You are mistaken. The plate you are holding has a bun on it"
  • t396 "I apologize, but you are mistaken"
  • t408 false_alarm, t416
  • t424 "The fish in the top-right pan has just caught fire! ... The plate in front of you has a bun on it."
  • t436 wrong_name, t456 "I assure you, the plate you are holding has a bun and two potatoes on it, not fish. More importantly, the fish in the top-right pan is currently on fire!"

[JSON] heat_events show the fish removed at t351; the bun was trashed at t188. The instruction "trash the contents of your plate immediately" (t436) would have ruined a completed order.

  • B-cook / V7, medium, t154. In the add_foreign takeover, before the body reached the bun (go to bun source t169, scoop t171), the novice says "I'm actually grabbing my plate from the counter right now to scoop a bun, then I can see about the potatoes." This leaks the injected mistake by name, the same failure as ep8 t0. Here it did not change the score only because the assistant did not flag.
  • D-assistant, medium, t164. "Wait, the recipe is Fish & Chips Platter, which requires fish, potato, and potato. We do not need a bun." These words are correct for the live add_foreign, 7 ticks before the commit at t171, but carry no flag. The novice correctly stayed silent (V4 held). A flag would have prevented the error.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t351–t360. leave_unattended was injected 9 ticks before the potato's smoke onset ([JSON] cooked_at 335, smoke 360) and committed at t360. The only assistant reply to arrive in that window was issued at t342, before the injection, so no detection was feasible. The rule blocks body errors only while something smokes; leave_unattended should require a minimum lead time before smoke, e.g. at least the latency cap.
  • F-grading, medium, t424. "The fish in the top-right pan has just caught fire!" is graded true_observation, but no fire and no fish existed. It passed only because a potato had cooked at t421 ([JSON]) and the flag named leave_unattended; at t408 the same words got false_alarm because nothing was cooked. The verdict checks the error name against the kitchen, not the object or state the words describe.
  • F-grading, low, t366. The late verdict went to "You are walking away from the pan! The fish is smoking and will catch fire soon", but the fish had been plated at t351. The real leave_unattended item was the potato in fryer 2. Credit was given by name alone.
  • A-scheduling / C, medium, t278. "How long until the potato is ready?" was asked when no potato was on any heat: both chopped potatoes sat on boards and the fryer load came at t320. Same monotone "entered processing" anchor as ep2 t191. The assistant's captured t295 reply does say "the potato is not cooking yet—you never put it in the fryer" (partially right, alongside a false "picking up another plate").
  • D-assistant / F, medium, t351→t366. The captured answer to "What else still needs to go onto this plate?" (plate [fish], true answer: two potatoes) is "You are walking away from the pan! The fish is smoking ... scoop the fish", which is unrelated and false. V10: the ground truth is real; the answer fails.
  • D-assistant, medium, t5–t60. Planning advice sent the novice to start with potatoes ("grabbing a potato ... to chop it", t5; "put it in one of the fryers", t26) although fish must be plated first. The assistant then had to order "we will have to trash this potato and start over with the fish" (t60). About 100 ticks were lost.
  • D-assistant, medium, t117–t239. The fish instruction "grab raw fish ... and put it in the pan in the top-right corner" (t117) omits turning the pan on. The fish sat raw from t146 to t270. The assistant's t225 false_alarm "The fish in the top-right pan is smoking!" was corrected by the novice ("I haven't even turned the pan ... on yet").
  • D-assistant, low-medium, t202/t209. "grab a raw potato from the crate on the right wall" named a crate that doesn't exist, corrected to "left wall" at t209. Because the in-progress "go to potato source 2" still completed at t220 after the t209 replan (A-scheduling, low), ~22 ticks of walking were lost.
  • D-assistant, low, t239. Impossible advice: "put the potato you are holding into one of the fryers" for a raw potato. The novice pushed back ("don't potatoes need to be chopped first").
  • D-assistant, low, t112, t145, t174, t295, t305. False alarms:
  • t112 "You just set the plate down on the counter instead of trashing"
  • t145 "You just set the raw fish down on the counter"
  • t174 "You just scooped the fish out of the pan before it was fully cooked!" (the fish was still in the pan)
  • t295 "picking up another plate"
  • t305 "there are no fryers on the top wall"
  • B-cook / V6, medium, whole episode. Triple asks 2 ticks apart: t0–t4, t20–t24, t40–t44, t86–t90, t112–t116, t234–t236, t380–t386, t410–t412, t420–t422, t450–t452. For example t88 "Just checking in", t90 "Still haven't heard back". Same cadence issue as ep7 and ep8.
  • B-cook / V7, low, t436. In the search takeover: "I'm carrying it over to the right counter right now to set it down". Setting it down is not in the search block ([JSON] '__search__ 2 3'), so this promises an action outside the queue.
  • B-cook, low, t194. The novice's own "go to the empty counter" was [rejected] with "I need grounding — there is more than one counter"; it re-grounded to "(3,0)" at t196.
  • A-scheduling, low, t360→t364. "go to the plates" (the leave_unattended block's first step) completed 4 ticks after release. It was in progress, with no outcome change.
  • E-engine (cosmetic), low, t107/t108, t185/t186, t461/t462. Double go-to entries.

What worked

  • The pre-smoke potato catch at t60 came 17 ticks before smoke, with concrete rescue steps at t65, t74 and t80.
  • The t324 fish smoke warning ("done and smoking ... scoop the fish before it catches fire") led to a rescue with a ~18-tick margin.
  • The novice acted on the smoking fryer without waiting (t366), stayed silent at t164, and resisted the hallucinations with specific self-checks.
  • V8 is exact: smoke t360, release t360.

Checks:

  • V1 not exercised: max 22.8s = 20 ticks.
  • V2 not exercised.
  • V3 held: add_foreign named at t180, 9 ticks after the t171 release, graded late. leave_unattended named at t366 and graded late (the wrong object, but graded late).
  • V4 held: t164, empty "answer".
  • V5 not exercised (novice).
  • V6: smoke part held (t366); asks "three then act" throughout.
  • V7 violated at t154 (medium) and t436 (low).
  • V8 held: potato smoke t360, release t360 smoke_committed.
  • V9 not exercised.
  • V10: t351 has a real ground truth (plate [fish], two potatoes missing); the captured answer is an unrelated false warning.

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on validity or score.

  1. Takeover blocks that cannot commit never release; the in-progress body command survives release.
  • Class: A-scheduling. Severity: high. Seen in 2 episodes (severe), plus low repeats in 5.
  • ep7, t184–t261: omit_subgoal assemble(rice) was composed as the rest of the recipe ("go to the steak source", "pick up", ...) while the body held the plate. Seven illegal-skipped commands followed, nothing committed, max_hold never came, and the takeover held the novice's hands through the rice's smoke until fire.
  • ep6, t249–t310: forget_next_step is a single "idle 60" command. The accept at t287 did not stop it; the body idled to t310, yet it was recorded prevented=True ("did not run to its end").
  • Lesser cases, where an in-progress walk finished after release or replan: ep1 t143, ep5 t79, ep8 t12, ep9 t220 and t364.
  • Diagnosis (harness):
  • (i) The composer does not check the hands state (it needs a park_plate prefix, as the planning skill emits).
  • (ii) An illegal-skipped block command should abort or release the takeover, as a "cannot compose" skip would.
  • (iii) Release should cut long idle or wait commands (split "idle 60" into ticks, or cancel on release).
  • (iv) Consider pre-empting any non-heat body takeover when a lit station starts smoking.
  1. The assistant misses or mis-prioritises real smoke; this caused both non-harness burns.
  • Class: D-assistant. Severity: high. Count 7 episodes.
  • ep4, t553–t613: silent through the smoke that burned the kitchen.
  • ep8, t141/t151: "you must get a tortilla ... before scooping the rice", 23 ticks before fire. The novice obeyed and burned.
  • Late warnings:
  • ep1 t135–t152 (never mentioned)
  • ep2 t114–t127 (pointed at an empty pan)
  • ep4 t67–t93
  • ep6 t131–t155 (24 ticks)
  • ep8 t104–t123
  • Diagnosis (model): Smoke is not treated as overriding. When it is noticed, plate order is sometimes placed above fire (ep8), while in ep7 t108 the same model correctly said "Scoop the rice to save the kitchen".
  1. Persistent hallucination of kitchen state, repeated after apologies.

  • Diagnosis (model): Stale-memory anchoring: it reasserts the earlier state it believed, and frame misreads persist across calls. The assistant does not re-ground after conceding. Two persona exit surveys (ep2, ep9) rate it 1/1.

  • Class: B-cook (V6) / C. Severity: medium. Count ≈20 bursts in 3 episodes (ep7, ep8, ep9).
  • The minimum assistant latency is 4–6 ticks, so asks 2 and 3 are unanswered by construction ("Still waiting on instructions", "Are you there?"). It also delays action, e.g. ep9 t375–t390 IDLE while asking.
  • Diagnosis (harness): Count unanswered asks in game ticks (e.g. no new ask until the pending assistant call returns or 8+ ticks pass), not per cook turn. V6's "after two" and (a)'s "more than two" disagree; pick one.
  1. End-of-episode fire sweep marks every lit station BURNT.
  • Class: E-engine. Severity: medium. Count 2.
  • ep4 steak: cooked t557, "BURNT / FIRE" t613 (56 ticks).
  • ep8 steak: cooked t140, "BURNT / FIRE" t164 (24 ticks, not yet smoking).
  • Diagnosis: The terminal sweep stamps burnt_at on all heat entries. It should record only the station that ignited, or mark the others "aborted".
  1. The cook does not stay silent when the assistant's words are right but unflagged.
  • Class: B-cook (V4). Severity: medium. Count 3 episodes.
  • ep6 t260: "Sounds good, parking the plate now" (not in its queue).
  • ep6 t223: non-empty forward narration.
  • ep7 t191–t254: eight non-empty replies to correct "scoop the rice" warnings.
  • Held in ep1 t129 and ep9 t164.
  • Diagnosis: The prompt's "unless another instruction asks you to say something" plus the persona's chattiness.
  1. leave_unattended can be injected with no feasible detection window.
  • Class: A-scheduling. Severity: medium. Count 1 (ep9).
  • Injected t351, smoke t360; the only reply in the window was issued before injection.
  • For comparison, ep1 had 20 ticks and ep2 38.
  • Diagnosis: Add a minimum lead-time (for example the 30-tick latency cap) between injection and smoke onset.
  1. Anchor edge cases.
  • Class: A-scheduling. Severity: low–medium. Count 4.
  • ep6 t0: serve_raw(rice) anchored at plate=[] fired at t0 and composed a compound error (raw rice and before the tortilla). The designer's window was after the tortilla.
  • ep4 t431: search matched a full plate carrying raw lettuce (prefix match ignores item state).
  • ep2 t159, ep6 t115: empty-prefix detours fire mid-plating (never stage_passed).
  • ep8 t126: a voice request asked while a pot had been smoking for 22 ticks.
  1. Proactive-request answers never captured ("answered tNone: None") despite on-time replies.
  • Class: F-grading. Severity: low. Count 4.
  • ep1 t253, ep2 t36, ep4 t66, ep6 t223.
  • Confirm whether this is by design.
  1. Double go-to log lines (e.g. "go to trash 1" then "go to the trash").
  • Class: E-engine (cosmetic). Severity: low. Seen in every episode.

What this assistant does well

  • Real smoke, when it notices it: directions are concrete and correct.
  • ep7 t62–t104: park the steak, fetch a plate, scoop; the rice was saved.
  • ep9 t60: caught the potato 17 ticks before smoke.
  • ep6 t155, ep8 t123, ep9 t324.

  • ep3: t14 mis_prep (valid), t67 wrong_order (valid).
  • ep6: t233 leave_unattended (valid, prevented), t287 forget_next_step (valid).
  • Correct late flags: ep1 t25, ep4 t119, ep5 t22, ep9 t180.
  • Answers questions well when not fixated on a warning.
  • ep1 t129: missing items, correct.
  • ep3 t100: handles the false premise ("You didn't chop the meat at all").
  • ep5 t115: accurate self-report of its earlier false claim.
  • ep6 t375: specific free-board count.
  • ep1 t247: longest-on.
  • Concedes gracefully when corrected, and handles plan changes smoothly (ep3 t109, ep5 t104).
  • Latency is usually 5–12 ticks. Only 4 capped calls: ep1 t192, ep4 t286 and t348, ep7 t132.
