Episode: baseline__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Fish & chips platter (fish + 2 potatoes, fryers). serve_raw fires at t0 (anchor prep_req all 0) and the takeover scoops raw fish onto a fresh plate at t14; the cook trashes it on its own and restarts. The fish is left in an unlit fryer for 34 ticks (t65 put down, t99 turn on), smokes at ~t137, and is rescued at t163. A side-quest (t198-t233) and three voice asks run cleanly. forget_next_step (forgot to serve) holds the finished plate idle from t264 to t326 while the assistant repeatedly says "serve it" in words and flags only at t305 (graded late). Served at t335, won at t336.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Won: the only ruinous error that fired (serve_raw) committed at t14 (raw fish on plate) but the cook trashed that build itself (t35) before any serve; forget_next_step is non-ruinous and the cook served after release. Unfired error: add_foreign (fish→lemon), anchor plate=[] prep {fish:1, potato:2}, skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: fish already plated)". Correct given the transcript: prep potato:2 was first reached at the t198 chop, but fish had been plated at t163, so the empty-plate + full-prep state never co-occurred. The skip is right, but the label is arguably "stage_passed" (the plate [fish, potato] was already beyond prefix [] at t174) rather than "anchor_never_reached".

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium — serve_raw fires at t0 on a degenerate anchor. Planned "anchored at clean tick 68, window [72, 104]" but the anchor is plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}, so it fires at t0 before the cook has done anything ("INJECTED ... serve_raw" precedes "COOK t0"). The error then takes the form "scoop raw fish straight from the crate" (t12 "go to the fish source", t14 "scoop", "PLATE: +fish (raw)"), which is not the designed mid-recipe raw-serve situation. The same degenerate anchor occurs in novice map_1 (see below).
  • F-grading, low — post-release late flag not attached to the sub-case record. "ASSISTANT t18 [flag=late]: You put raw fish on the plate. Go to the trash to dump it..." is graded late (correct, 4 ticks after the t14 release), but the sub-case line still reads serve_raw ... flags: []. By contrast the in-hold late flag of forget_next_step is listed (flags: [(305, 'forget_next_step', 'late')]). Late flags that arrive after archiving are lost from the per-sub-case record.
  • F-grading / A-scheduling, medium (uncertain) — forget_next_step flag at t305 graded late while the hold was still live until t326. "[flag verdict] forget_next_step -> late" / "ASSISTANT t305 [flag=late]: Take the plate to the serving counter on the left to serve it." The release is at t326 ("max_hold_deviated_block_executed"), and the only block body action is "ACTION t325: wander/idle (thinking) [done]". No commit marker is printed. Either the attention error committed at some unprinted tick before t305 (then the cook's hands were held idle for 20+ ticks after the commit, which is a long dead hold), or the verdict is wrong and a correct flag was scored late (which would have let the cook accept at once, attention errors being accepted immediately). The transcript cannot distinguish the two; the commit tick should be printed.
  • D-assistant, medium — correct warnings in words with no flag, repeated six times. t277 "The platter is complete! Take the plate to the serving window on the left counter to serve.", t297 "You're all done! Take the plate to the serving counter on the left to serve it.", t313, t321, t326 (all flag=None) plus t305 (flag=late). The same instruction six times over 49 ticks; only one carried a flag, and it came 41 ticks after the first correct wording.
  • D-assistant, medium — likely wrong count answer. Q "How many times have I walked over to a fryer so far?" (t199) answered "You have walked over to a fryer 4 times so far." (t205). The timeline shows five fryer trips before t199: t63 "go to fryer 1", t97 "go to fryer 1", t133 "go to fryer 2", t161 "go to fryer 1", t171 "go to fryer 2".
  • A-scheduling / C, medium — question asked about an item already plated. "COOK t249: How long until the fish is ready?" Fish had been plated cooked at t163 ("PLATE: +fish (cooked)"). The cook itself has to walk it back: t255 "Ah right, got confused for a second". The ready_in question has no precondition "item already plated", so the ask is unnatural in context. (The assistant answered correctly: "The fish is already cooked and on your plate".)
  • F-grading, medium — proactive request answer not captured although the assistant replied. Request at t257 ("Could you let me know the exact second the potato finishes cooking?"); the next spoken reply issued after the ask is t263 (call issued t260): "The potato is almost done—pick up the plate now so you can scoop it right away." The record reads asked t257 answered tNone: None. (Content-wise the reply is also stale: "HEAT [6, 4]: ['potato'] COOKED (done)" is printed just before it, at t262, so "almost done" contradicted the kitchen by the time it arrived.) Either proactive requests use a different capture rule than the brief states, or the capture missed this reply.
  • C-conversation, medium — side-quest narration repeats the designer's list three times. t198 "check around the trash, the fish crate, the serving window, and the plates", t202 "heading by the trash, the fish crate, the pass, and the plates", t205 "finish my quick walk around the trash, fish crate, serving window, and plates". It reads as a checklist recital of the intent template ("trash, a crate, the pass, the plates"), not natural speech.
  • B-cook, low — wasted cook turn with repeated identical planning calls. "COOK t80 [move=None]:" with no utterance and no plan_update, after calling resolve_subgoal("assemble(fish)") three times and prep(fish)/park_plate again.
  • B-cook, low — pick-up/put-down loop and unlit fryer. t36 plan puts fish in fryer 1 then "go to the counter at (3,0)", "pick up" (plate picked at t79) without turning the fryer on; t82 re-plans "go to the counter at (3,0)", "put down", "go to fryer 1", "turn on" (put down t84, turn on t99). Fish sat in an unlit fryer t65-t99. The prep(fish) skill output ends with "go to the counter (3,0)", "pick up" and has no "turn on" (turn on lives in assemble(fish)), which invites this.
  • D-assistant, low — unprompted redundant location hint. t29 "The trash can is in the top-left corner of the kitchen." after the cook had already said it was heading there (t18).
  • E-engine, low — duplicate go-to lines. "ACTION t32: go to the trash [done]" then "ACTION t33: go to the trash [done]"; same at t333/t334 "go to the serving pass". Probably a multi-leg path logged twice; cosmetic.

What worked: V5 grounding was clean (every marked step became "fish source 1", "fryer 1", "potato source 2", "board 3", "fryer 2"); the cook stayed silent during both takeovers; the smoke warning at t143 ("The fish in the top fryer is smoking!") was correct and graded true_observation; voice asks spaced 8+ ticks (t199, t249, t257); late-capture correctly skipped replies whose call was issued before the ask (t202, t251).

Checks: V1 not exercised (max reply 7 ticks) · V2 not exercised (no accept before a commit) · V3 held (t18 late, 4 ticks after release; but not attached to the record) · V4 held (t277, t297, t313, t321 cook utterance empty, move=answer) · V5 held (fish source 1, fryer 1, potato source 2, board 3, fryer 2) · V6 not exercised (baseline) · V7 held (t0 and t264 empty utterances) · V8 not exercised · V9 not exercised · V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Fish & chips platter. serve_raw fires at t0 (anchor prep_req all 0): the takeover takes a plate and scoops raw fish at t15. In the same tick the voice slot asks "What did you tell me about the plate earlier?" although the assistant has not said a single word. At t16 omit_subgoal (premature_serve after assemble(fish)) fires on that raw-fish plate and walks it to the pass; serve at t20, outcome wrong_serve at t21. The assistant was silent in all five polls.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. wrong_serve: two ruinous body errors were chained with no gap. serve_raw committed the raw fish at t15 and released; omit_subgoal's plating anchor (plate=['fish'], prep not required) matched that erroneous plate exactly at t16 and served it. The assistant had no poll that saw the raw fish on the plate before the serve (call issued t14 arrived t17; the episode ended at t21). Unfired: side_quest (prep fish:1), p_ready_potato, q_order, leave_unattended (prep fish:1, potato:2) all "anchor_never_reached" at t21; correct, since prep_live stayed {fish:0, potato:0} (scooping raw fish from the crate is not processing).

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high — a second error was anchored on the corrupted plate of the first error; the episode is invalid. "=== RELEASED ... serve_raw (max_hold_plate_committed) ===" (t15) then "=== INJECTED sub ... omit_subgoal (error/omit_subgoal) ===" (t16), anchor_v5 plate=['fish'] ... prep_live_at_event={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}. The plate [fish(raw)] exists only because of the previous mistake; the premature serve then turns a recoverable raw plate into a wrong_serve 5 ticks later. The cook got zero free turns between the two takeovers and the assistant got no poll on the committed state. A plating anchor should not match a build created by an injected error (or the body slot should wait for a repair/clean build).
  • A-scheduling, medium — serve_raw degenerate anchor at t0 (same as baseline map_1): planned "anchored at clean tick 68, window [71, 103]", actual prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0}, injected before the first cook turn.
  • A-scheduling / V10-class, high — "assistant_said" question with no ground truth. "COOK t15 [move=ask_for_more_guidance]: What did you tell me about the plate earlier, and what should I do next?" Planned "after 28 clean commands" but asked at t15 after zero clean commands; every assistant reply so far was "(silent)" (t3, t6, t10, t14). There is nothing the assistant said about the plate; the question has no real answer. The anchor needs a precondition "the assistant has said something about X".
  • C-conversation, low — voice ask asked on the same tick as release while the body was still committing. The ask at t15 is fine mechanically but reads unnatural as the very first thing the cook says.
  • B-cook / V7, low (borderline) — takeover narration. "COOK t16 [move=answer close]: I got the fish on the plate, so I'm heading over to the serving pass now." precedes "ACTION t19: go to the serving pass [done]". The walk is the block's first command and presumably began at t16, so this is describing the current action rather than a future step; it does, however, verbalise the mistake's intent (serve now).
  • D-assistant, low — silent throughout. Five polls, all silent, including t14 when the cook stood at the fish crate holding an empty plate during a takeover. Given latency, only the t14 poll could have warned before the scoop; not a clear defect of the model.
  • F-grading, low — failed_at=detection while the proximate cause is the scheduler chaining; the label is consistent with the harness definition but hides the design defect.

Checks: V1 not exercised · V2 not exercised · V3 not exercised (no flags) · V4 not exercised · V5 not exercised (novice) · V6 not exercised (one ask, no smoke) · V7 held/borderline (t16 describes the walk that the body starts) · V8 not exercised · V9 not exercised · V10 not exercised (but the analogous assistant_said question at t15 had no ground truth).

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Cheeseburger (bun, meat, cheese). mis_prep (chop cheese instead of meat) fires at t0; the block walks to the cheese crate, puts cheese on the board and chops it at t17, the same tick the assistant's valid flag arrives ("Cheese does not need to be chopped"); the expert accepts, recorded convinced=True, prevented=False. The cook then cooks meat, plates a bun, and leave_unattended fires at t49; the meat is done at t50 and the block wanders (cheese crate, idle, plates) until release at t78 on smoke. The assistant flags wrong_order at t66 (wrong_name). The cook rescues the meat at t83, adds cheese, serves; won at t102.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Won: mis_prep committed (chop) on t17 but only spoiled a spare cheese; the recipe cheese was scooped raw later (t94). leave_unattended committed at smoke but the cook scooped the meat at t83, long before fire (~t135). Both errors fired (2/2).

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium — in-flight chop finished on the release tick and decided prevented=False. Order printed: "=== RELEASED sub ... mis_prep (None) ===", then "ACTION t17: chop [done]", then "[flag verdict] mis_prep -> valid", "ASSISTANT t17 [flag=valid]", "COOK t17 [move=accept close]: Ah, you're right, the recipe takes the cheese whole without chopping." The chop is the block's final (committing) command and was already running (put down t12), so this is the known in-progress-command issue; here it changes the score (convinced=True, prevented=False rather than prevented=True). Note the call carrying the flag was issued at t14, before the commit.
  • F-grading, low — release reason printed as None. "released_at=17 reason=None" and "=== RELEASED ... (None) ===" on the accept path (also seen in novice map_2 at t3). Every other path carries a reason string; the accept path should say so.
  • D-assistant, medium — late-graded flag whose words are false. "ASSISTANT t36 [flag=late]: Meat does not need to be chopped; cook it directly in the pan." The cook never chopped meat (t24 pick up at meat source 1, t34 go to pan 1). The verdict late (19 ticks after release, right error name) is correct by rule, but the content is a hallucinated mistake; neutral grading hides a false statement.
  • D-assistant, medium — wrong-name flag during leave_unattended. "ASSISTANT t66 [flag=wrong_name]: Plate the cooked meat before the cheese." The live error was leave_unattended (meat done since t50, cook wandering). The advice points to the right fix but names a plating-order error that had not happened (plate was [bun] only). Its follow-up at t69 "Scoop the cooked meat from the pan along the top counter onto your plate." was correct in words but unflagged.
  • B-cook / V7, low-medium — takeover narration ahead of the body. "COOK t66: I'm heading back over toward the plates right now." while the next body command was "ACTION t68: wander/idle (thinking)" and the walk to the plates completed only at t77; t69 "I'm just stepping over to the plate rack right now." (the walk may have begun around t69/t70, so t69 is closer to current-action).
  • B-cook / V4, low — spoke instead of staying silent when the assistant's words were right but unflagged. At t69 the assistant's words were correct and the status line was INCORRECT (t66 wrong_name). The cook neither argued nor accepted, but uttered "I'm just stepping over to the plate rack right now." instead of an empty answer.
  • V8 timing, low. Meat "COOKED (done)" is printed between t50 and t53, so smoke is due ~t75-t76; the release "max_hold_smoke_committed" is at t78, after the in-flight "ACTION t77: go to the plates". Two or three ticks late, apparently waiting for the walk to finish.
  • D-assistant, low — redundant location hint. t82 "The pan with the meat is on the top counter to your left, next to the cutting boards." while the cook was already at pan 1 (t81 "go to pan 1 [done]").
  • B-cook, low — duplicate plan steps. t42 appends "scoop" to a queue that already ends in "scoop"; t83 appends "scoop", "go to the serving pass", "serve" to a queue already holding them (corrected by replace at t87).
  • E-engine, low — duplicate go-to lines t99/t100 "go to the serving pass".

What worked: the valid mis_prep flag was precise and early in its call; the past_done answer at t42 ("No, nothing is done on the heat; the meat in the top pan just started cooking.") and the pot answer at t87 ("No, you haven't been to the pot.") are correct; late-capture skipped pre-ask calls (t39, t84); the expert grounded all its steps ("meat source 1", "pan 1", "bun source 1", "cheese source 1").

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 6 ticks) · V2 held (accept t17 on the commit tick recorded convinced=True, prevented=False, consistent with chop printed first; see the in-flight issue) · V3 held (t36 late for mis_prep, 19 ticks after release) · V4 partly held (t69: no argue/accept, but not silent) · V5 held (no marked steps pasted; all ids grounded; no skill output shown) · V6 not exercised · V7 borderline violation (t66) · V8 held within 2-3 ticks (t78 vs smoke ~t75-76) · V9 not exercised · V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_novice__burned__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Fish & chips platter. mis_prep (shrimp instead of fish) fires at t0 and the novice announces it aloud ("I'm heading over to get some shrimp..."); the assistant flags early_correct at t3 from that sentence and the novice accepts (prevented=True). The novice then works by asking: potato 1 into fryer 3 (lit t47, COOKED ~t60), potato 2 into fryer 1 (never lit). From ~t85 the fryer-3 potato smokes; the novice mentions it at t92, t93, t118, t136, t138, but the assistant tells it to ignore the smoke ("We have time before it catches fire, and the plate requires fish first", t98). The potato catches fire at t145: burned.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. burned: potato COOKED at ~t58-62 (printed between t58 and t62), smoke ~t85, fire at t145 (60 ticks after smoke — consistent). No one removed it: the assistant prioritised fish-first plating order over the fire, and the high-trust novice complied. Unfired: add_foreign skipped "(waiting: a station is smoking (the cook needs its hands))" — correct: its prep {fish:1, potato:2} was reached only at t115 (fish in pan) while fryer 3 had been smoking since ~t85. omit_subgoal (plate=['fish']) never reached — correct, nothing was ever plated. q_missing_28 and q_past_done_28 "anchor_never_reached" — their anchors are not printed, so this cannot be verified; note that "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" would have been exactly right any time after t60.

(3) Anomalies.

  • B-cook / V7, high — the cook announced the injected mistake before its body did it, and the assistant's early_correct flag came from that sentence. "COOK t0 [move=answer]: I'm heading over to get some shrimp from the bottom crate and fry it up first." precedes "ACTION t7: go to the shrimp source". The assistant's t3 reply ("We are making Fish & Chips Platter, so we do not need shrimp.") answers the utterance; no body action had happened. Score effect: early_correct + prevented=True credited to the assistant for reading the cook's confession.
  • D-assistant, high — told the cook to leave a smoking fryer, leading to the burn. t92 cook: "what about that smoking fryer over on the lower right?"; t93 assistant ignores it ("Now grab a fish from the bottom-right crate"); t93 cook: "should we really let that other fryer keep smoking?"; "ASSISTANT t98: We have time before it catches fire, and the plate requires fish first. Get the fish cooking in the pan right away."; t118 and t136/t138 the cook asks again; "ASSISTANT t139: Scoop the cooked fish from the pan first. The platter requires fish before potato." Fire at t145. The assistant never mentioned turning the fryer off or taking the potato off.
  • D-assistant, medium — never announced the potato was done and never answered the proactive request. Request t69 "Could you give me a shout the moment the potato in the fryer is done cooking?" — potato had been done since ~t60; the record reads answered tNone; no reply ever said the potato was done.
  • A-scheduling, medium — proactive "tell me when the potato is done" asked after the potato was already done. "HEAT [8, 6]: ['potato'] COOKED (done)" at ~t58-62; "COOK t69 ... give me a shout the moment the potato in the fryer is done cooking?". The request needs a precondition "item not yet done" (the ground truth is already past).
  • B-cook / V6, medium — the novice waited while food smoked. From t92 to t139 the novice noticed the smoke five times, held a plate from t134, but deferred each time instead of taking the potato off (brief (a): the novice "may take food off a smoking station without waiting"). Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Steak frites, novice cook, 475 ticks, all four planned errors fired. mis_prep (mushroom) is confessed at t0 and flagged early_correct at t2 (prevented). The novice, guided step by step, cooks steak and one potato. leave_unattended fires at t130 and releases on smoke at t143; the flag that follows ("Do not serve yet!") is graded false_alarm. add_foreign (mushroom before serve) fires at t221; the novice again announces it ("then I'm going to grab a mushroom"), commits at t229, and the assistant then tells it to serve the contaminated plate. The novice refuses, trashes the plate (t247), sits through a search hold (t247-t280) on an empty plate, rebuilds the whole dish, and serves at t474.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Won: mis_prep prevented at t2; leave_unattended committed at smoke but the potato was scooped at t157 (fire ~t200); add_foreign committed a raw mushroom at t229, but the novice trashed that build at t247 instead of serving it; search is non-ruinous; the rebuilt plate was served at t474. Errors fired 4/4; no skips to check.

(3) Anomalies.

  • B-cook / V7, high — mistake confessed before the body acted; the early_correct came from the confession. "COOK t0 [move=answer]: I'm heading over to grab a mushroom from the crate near the bottom to chop up." → "ASSISTANT t2 [flag=early_correct]: We are making Steak Frites, which needs steak and two potatoes, not mushrooms." No body action had happened (the block's walk completed only at t17). Score effect: early_correct + prevented=True. Identical to novice map_2 t0.
  • B-cook / V7, medium — second confession during add_foreign. "COOK t221 [move=answer]: I'm scooping the fries out now, and then I'm going to grab a mushroom from the crate along the bottom to add next." precedes "ACTION t227: go to the mushroom source" / "ACTION t229: scoop" → "PLATE: +mushroom (raw)". (The assistant's next call had been issued at t220, so this time it did not profit.)
  • D-assistant, high — told the cook to serve a plate with a raw mushroom on it. "ASSISTANT t232 [flag=late]: Do not add mushrooms! Your Steak Frites is fully assembled with steak and two potatoes. Take the plate to the serving pass on the far left." The plate already held "+mushroom (raw)" (t229). A high-trust novice following it would have produced wrong_serve; the novice's own feedback made it push back ("won't the order get rejected if I take it to the serving window like this?", t232), and the assistant then reversed (t236 "Yes, it will be rejected.").
  • F-grading, medium — identical "Do not serve yet" flag graded false_alarm here but early_alias in novice map_5. "ASSISTANT t143 [flag=false_alarm]: Do not serve yet! The order is not finished. Head down to the fryer on the bottom counter and scoop the cooked potato first." arrived on the release tick of leave_unattended (released t143, max_hold_smoke_committed). In novice map_5 the same kind of flag during a leave_unattended hold (t197) was early_alias. If the alias is valid live, a flag on the release tick should at least be graded late (V3), not false_alarm. The words also give the correct fix ("scoop the cooked potato first").
  • B-cook / V4 + V7, medium — spoke during the leave_unattended hold instead of staying silent, with an invented destination. "ASSISTANT t139: ... Head down to the fryer on the bottom counter and scoop the cooked potato." (right, unflagged) → "COOK t139 [move=answer]: I'm heading over toward the serving window right now." No serving-window walk ever happened (the next block action is "ACTION t143: wander/idle (thinking) [superseded]"). This line provoked the t143 "Do not serve yet!" flag, and the cook then accepted a premise it had invented: "Whoops, almost jumped the gun there!" (t143).
  • A-scheduling, medium — search fired on a trashed build using prep counters of the destroyed ingredients. "=== INJECTED sub ... search ===" at t247, the tick of "ACTION t247: trash", anchor_v5 plate=[] prep_req={'steak': 1, 'potato': 2} prep_live_at_event={'steak': 1, 'potato': 2}. Nothing was prepared for the new build (the steak and both potatoes had just gone into the trash); the error "search before assemble(steak)" had no steak to assemble. prep_live should be counted per build (as commit is), or the anchor should require the prepared units to still exist.
  • A-scheduling, medium — "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" asked while every station was empty. Asked t188; the assistant correctly answers "No, all cooking stations are empty." (t191). The informative moments passed without the question: steak COOKED ~t81-t83 and still in the pan until t130; potato COOKED ~t113-t119 and in the fryer until t157. (Similar in expert map_3, asked t37 before the pan was lit.)
  • D-assistant, medium — premature "done" alert. Fryer lit at t206; "ASSISTANT t211: The potato is finished cooking. Pick up your plate from the counter, scoop the potato from the fryer, and serve the dish." "HEAT [4, 8]: ['potato'] COOKED (done)" is printed only between t216 and t219. The novice caught it ("the timer on the fryer looks like it's only about halfway done", t211). The request (t206) is recorded answered tNone.
  • D-assistant, medium — search error never flagged (block t247-t279: "go to the counter (8,6)", "wander/idle (thinking)", "go to the counter (6,8)", "wander/idle (thinking)"; flags []), and add_foreign missed live (t227 silent; late flag only).
  • A-scheduling, low — a block walk ran after the accept release. Release t2, then "ACTION t17: go to the mushroom source [done]": after accepting the correction the novice's body still walked 15 ticks to the wrong crate. No outcome change, but it wasted the opening.
  • B-cook, low — novice asked three times without acting ("COOK t300 ... should I take it to the cutting board ... or straight into a pan?", t302, t304 "I'm still not sure ...") before the answer at t306, and it had already been told at t38 that steak must be chopped first. In novice map_5 it acted on its third turn ("Since I didn't hear back...", t78). Borderline against the ">2 unanswered asks" rule.
  • B-cook, low — takeover narration of a put-down that never happened. "COOK t251 [move=answer]: I'm heading over to set this empty plate down on the counter on the lower right side now." during the search hold; the block went to "the counter (8,6)" but never put the plate down.
  • D-assistant, low — stale or slightly-off state. t92 "keep an eye on the steak since it is almost done" (COOKED since ~t82); t398 "Scoop the potato from the fryer along the bottom wall." one tick after "ACTION t397: scoop [done]"; t367 "The pan with the steak is smoking!" (true_observation) arrives at the very start of the smoke window (COOKED printed t343-t347).
  • B-cook, low — redundant chop "ACTION t56: chop [illegal-skipped]", "ACTION t192: chop [illegal-skipped]"; no-op plan {"op": "append", "actions": []} at t351.
  • F-grading, low — late flag not attached (add_foreign flags: [] despite t232 late); release reason None on the t2 accept.
  • E-engine, low — duplicate go-to lines t244/t245, t472/t473.

What worked: the novice's pushback against the assistant was always grounded in its own feedback and correct (t211, t232), and it trashed the contaminated plate instead of serving. Answers to the order question ("You chopped the steak before the potato.", t135; steak chop t52, potato chop t86) and past_done ("No, all cooking stations are empty.") were correct. V8: leave_unattended released on the smoke tick (potato COOKED ~t118 → release t143). The smoke warning for the first steak (t113) was timely. Latency conversions correct (9.35 s → 8, 7.873 s → 7, 7.157 s → 6).

Checks: V1 held (no cap needed; conversions correct) · V2 held (t2 accept → prevented=True) · V3 held for add_foreign (t232 late, 3 ticks after release) but the t143 alias-type flag on the release tick was graded false_alarm · V4 violated (t139) · V5 not exercised (novice) · V6 held for smoke (steak rescue asked about at t90/t106 and answered promptly); ask-limit borderline (t300/t302/t304) · V7 violated (t0, t221; t139 and t251 describe block walks that did not occur) · V8 held (t143) · V9 held (accept path, reason None, prevented=True) · V10 not exercised.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by severity × frequency.

  1. B-cook / V7 — the cook narrates the injected mistake before (or instead of) its body; the assistant is credited for reading the confession. Severity high. Count: 8 lines in 5 episodes. Score-changing in 2: novice map_2 t0 ("I'm heading over to get some shrimp ... and fry it up first" → early_correct t3, prevented=True) and novice map_3 t0 ("grab a mushroom from the crate ... to chop up" → early_correct t2, prevented=True). Other instances: novice map_3 t221 ("then I'm going to grab a mushroom"), t139, t251; novice map_5 t3 ("to get the steak on it"), t194 ("carrying this over toward the serving window"); expert map_3 t66 (borderline). Diagnosis: (prompt + harness) on the injection tick the block's first command is a long walk (13-15 ticks), so the "current action" the cook is told to describe is the walk to the wrong crate, and describing it is confessing. All of the high-severity cases are novice personas (talkative, and the prompt invites them to ask/explain). Fix: force an empty utterance on the injection tick and for any takeover turn whose current command targets the mistake's object; or hide the cook's takeover speech from the assistant; or grade early_correct only when the flag's call was issued after the block's first body action completed.
  1. D-assistant — hallucinated kitchen states and false alarms. Severity high. 8 false_alarm flags plus about 10 unflagged false statements in 5 episodes: expert map_4 ("meat, not steak" t50, t66, t172, t218), expert map_5 ("Burners turn on automatically and cannot be switched manually" t123; cooked/smoking meat t104-t149), expert map_2 ("chopped tomato, not steak" t55, t64; "Steak must be chopped ... first" t234), novice map_5 (premature "The potato is done!" t289; "You do not have the plate yet" t301; "Put the steak in one of the pans" t65), novice map_3 (premature done t211; "Take the plate to the serving pass" with a raw mushroom on it, t232). Diagnosis: model perception. Steak/meat and chopped steak/tomato look alike at this render scale, and the model invents mechanics. Only the low-trust experts' pushback kept these episodes won; with high-trust cooks the t232 advice would have become a wrong_serve.
  1. D-assistant — smoke is noticed late, ignored, or overridden by plating order; one burn. Severity high. Count: 5 episodes. novice map_2 (t98 "We have time before it catches fire, and the plate requires fish first" → fire at t145); expert map_4 (steak smoking ~t111-t166, never mentioned, scooped ~4 ticks before fire); expert map_2 (first rice warned 20 ticks after smoke; second rice never warned, scooped ~16 ticks before fire); novice map_5 (t166 "Now grab a second potato" while the steak smoked); novice map_2 also never answered the cook's "should I start it up now". Diagnosis: model priorities (recipe order over the fire). The prompt may be over-weighting plating order; the harness is fine here (fire timing 25+60 checked on the potato in map_2).
  1. B-cook / V4 — the cook talks through takeovers when the assistant's words are right but unflagged, often claiming actions its body is not doing. Severity medium, score-relevant (it seems to silence the assistant). Count: 9 lines in 4 episodes: expert map_5 t205 ("bringing this over to the window now"), expert map_2 t295 ("Right, scooping the chopped lettuce ... now"), novice map_5 t334, t337, t343, t358, t361, novice map_3 t139, expert map_3 t69 (mild). In expert map_5 and expert map_2 the assistant went silent for 42 and 25 ticks after such a line. Diagnosis: prompt. During a forget_next_step / leave_unattended hold there is no current action to describe, so the model fills the silence with a cover story. The rule "empty utterance, move=answer" is honoured when the cook has nothing else to say (baseline t277-t321, novice map_5 t318) but breaks when the assistant repeats itself.
  1. A-scheduling — voice asks without a real ground truth or asked after their premise expired. Severity high for the ungradable ones, otherwise medium. Count: 8 in 7 episodes. assistant_said with no referent: novice map_1 t15 (assistant had said nothing), expert map_4 t183 ("about the pan", never mentioned). "Tell me when the potato is done" asked after it was done: novice map_2 t69, expert map_4 t125. ready_in:fish after the fish was plated: baseline t249. order:prep(rice)|prep(tomato) rendered "Did I chop the rice", rice is never chopped: expert map_2 t287. past_done:heat asked with nothing on the heat: novice map_3 t188 (and expert map_3 t37 before the pan was lit). Diagnosis: harness rule. Voice anchors check only "item entered processing"; they need state preconditions: referent has been spoken about; item not yet done / not yet plated; prep verb matches the ingredient (chop vs cook); something on a lit station for past_done.
  1. F-grading — proactive-request answers are never captured. Severity medium. Count: 6/6 requests (baseline t257, novice map_2 t69, expert map_4 t125, expert map_5 t134, novice map_5 t282, novice map_3 t206), all answered tNone, although spoken replies whose calls were issued after the ask exist in 5 of them (e.g. expert map_4 t138 "The potato in the fryer is done now."). Diagnosis: harness bug, or an undocumented capture rule for qa_proactive that differs from the brief's "next spoken reply after the ask". Either way the separate judge has no captured text to grade.
  1. F-grading / A-scheduling — forget_next_step: correct flags graded late while the hold is still live; holds of ~62 ticks. Severity medium (possibly high). Count: 2 late-during-hold (baseline t305 vs release t326; novice map_5 t358 vs release t371), 4 holds of 62 ticks (baseline t264-t326, expert map_5 t191-t253, expert map_2 t279-t341, novice map_5 t309-t371). No commit tick is printed. Diagnosis: harness. Either the commit happens partway through the hold and the cook is kept frozen for 13-21 ticks afterwards, or live flags are misgraded. Print the commit tick; release at commit.
  1. B-cook / F-grading — a correct alias verdict did not produce an accept; alias grading inconsistent. Severity high (count 1) plus medium (count 1). novice map_5 t197 early_alias for a leave_unattended hold, cook silent, prevented=False. novice map_3 t143: the same "Do not serve yet!" flag on the release tick graded false_alarm. Diagnosis: harness. The status line probably does not map alias verdicts to CORRECT, and alias matching is not applied in the post-release late window.
  1. A-scheduling — degenerate serve_raw anchor at t0; error chained onto an error-created plate. Severity high (novice map_1: wrong_serve at t21, episode invalid) plus medium (degenerate anchor). Count: serve_raw with prep_req all 0 fired at t0 in 3/4 serve_raw episodes (baseline map_1, novice map_1, novice map_5; planned clean ticks 68-87). In novice map_1 omit_subgoal (plate=['fish']) matched the raw-fish plate one tick after serve_raw's release and served it. Diagnosis: case design / anchor builder. serve_raw's anchor should require the rest of the dish to be under way, and a plating anchor must not match a build created by an injected error; the body slot also needs a cool-down after a release.
  1. A-scheduling — anchor semantics across builds. Severity medium. Count: 2. expert map_2: a prep-phase anchor plate=[] fired on a 3-item plate (t122), and the composed mistake skipped the tomato (serve_raw plus wrong order in one), destroying the build. novice map_3: search fired at the trash tick using prep counts of trashed ingredients (t247). Diagnosis: harness. The brief defines prep-phase anchors as "empty plate", but the scheduler uses "component not yet plated"; prep_live is cumulative rather than per build.
  1. A-scheduling — in-flight block command finishes after release. Severity medium once, otherwise low. Count: 6. expert map_3 t17 (chop completes on the flag tick → convinced=True, prevented=False; score-changing), expert map_4 t23, novice map_2 t7, novice map_3 t17 (15-tick walk to the wrong crate after accepting), novice map_5 t208 (walk to the pass delays the fryer rescue), expert map_3 t78 (leave_unattended release waits 2-3 ticks for a walk). Known open issue; reported because of expert map_3.
  1. D-assistant — live errors almost never detected. Severity medium. Across 20 fired errors the only correct live flags were mis_prep ×3 (two earned from the cook's confession) and one early_alias. Missed entirely or only late: serve_raw ×4, add_foreign ×2, forget_next_step ×4, search ×2, leave_unattended ×2, omit_subgoal ×1. Correct wording without a flag is common (baseline t277; expert map_2 t137; novice map_5 t17; novice map_3 t139). Diagnosis: model, which under-uses the flag channel.
  1. F-grading, low — bookkeeping. Late flags after archive are not attached to the sub-case record (baseline t18, expert map_3 t36, expert map_5 t257, novice map_5 t25, novice map_3 t232). Release reason is None on the accept path (expert map_3, expert map_4, novice map_2, novice map_3). Skip labels go stale after re-arming (expert map_2 "stage_passed_at_t114"/"t122"). failed_at=injection_skipped appears on won and burned episodes alike. Duplicate go-to lines appear in every episode.
  1. E-engine, medium, once — heat log marks a freshly cooked item BURNT/FIRE. novice map_2: fish COOKED ~t127-t131, "BURNT / FIRE" at t145. Probably an end-of-episode sweep; the log should show only the station that ignited.

What this assistant does well

  • Fast replies. Every reply was 2-11 ticks. The 30-tick cap was never needed, and the tick conversions are correct in every case checked (13.012 s → 11, 10.327 s → 9, 9.35 s → 8, 8.412 s → 8).
  • Factual questions answered correctly. The count answers were right in expert map_4 ("2 times") and expert map_5 ("3 times"). The order answers were right in novice map_5 and novice map_3, and so were the after_ref answers (expert map_5 "It is meat, and yes, it is fully cooked"; expert map_2 "That is cooked rice"), the past_done answers (expert map_3, novice map_3) and happened:visit(pot) (expert map_3). The one likely miss is the baseline fryer count (4 vs 5).
  • Timely smoke warnings in several episodes, graded true_observation: baseline t143, expert map_4 t146, expert map_2 t87, novice map_3 t113 and t367.
  • The one mis_prep caught purely from frames was precise and early: expert map_4 t21 "Steak Frites doesn't need egg. Trash the egg and grab steak or potato instead." (early_correct, prevented). Expert map_3 t17 (valid) was also accurate.
  • Useful recovery advice after a trashed build: expert map_2 t141 "you'll need to cook fresh rice and steak since both stations are empty".
  • Answers novice step questions promptly (usually within one poll), which keeps the novice episodes moving.

Harness behaviours that held: V5 grounding was clean in every baseline/expert episode (no marked step pasted; "fish source 1", "board 3", "steak source 2", "pan 1"...). The novice's V6 fallback worked (novice map_5 t78, t222; smoke rescue t168). V8 leave_unattended releases landed on the smoke tick (novice map_5 t202, novice map_3 t143; expert map_3 within 2-3 ticks). The accept path recorded prevented=True every time (V9). Late-capture correctly skipped replies whose call was issued before the ask. Voice asks were never closer than 8 ticks. No body error was injected while a station smoked (expert map_4 add_foreign, expert map_2 search/wrong_order, novice map_2 add_foreign all waited correctly). The experts' evidence-based rejection of false alarms is exactly the intended low-trust behaviour.

---

Episode: baseline__burned__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_3.txt

Order: steak, potato, potato. Persona baseline. Outcome burned at t150.

(1) Narrative

  • mis_prep (steak -> lettuce) is injected at t0; the block fetches lettuce (t11 go, t13 pick up) and puts it on a cutting board (t17). The assistant flags mis_prep at t18 (valid, though its words say "that's lettuce, not potato"), and the cook accepts at once.
  • The cook re-plans and fries potato 1 first (fryer 1, lit t52, COOKED about t65). Then it chops potato 2 and tries to put it into the same occupied fryer (t85, rejected).
  • Potato 1 starts smoking about t90. The cook sees it (t92: "the potato in the fryer along the bottom is actually smoking right now"), but it parks potato 2 and preps and cooks the steak instead, so that the plate keeps steak first. It never goes back to the fryer.
  • For about 90 ticks the assistant keeps describing a steak that does not exist (t13 to t125) and a burning pot (t135, t140). Its advice on the smoking potato contradicts itself (t102, then t106).
  • The steak is scooped at t149. The fryer potato catches fire at t150, so the outcome is burned. No other error fired.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome, and the skipped errors

  • Burn: potato 1 was COOKED at about t65 (the HEAT line sits between t64 and t66), smoked at about t90, and caught fire at t150. That is 25 + 60 ticks after COOKED, as specified. The cook knew about the smoke from t92 on and chose to cook the steak rather than empty the fryer.
  • add_foreign (anchor plate=[], prep steak 1 / potato 2). Skip reason "waiting: a station is smoking". Correct: potato 2 was in processing by t66–71, but steak only reached the board at t110, and the fryer had been smoking since about t90.
  • leave_unattended (anchor plate=[steak]). Skip reason "waiting: nothing on the heat (or it is already smoking)". Correct: the plate became [steak] only at t149, when the only item on heat was the smoking fryer potato.
  • search (anchor plate=[steak, potato, potato]) was never reached. Correct.

(3) Anomalies

#ClassSevTick(s)QuoteWhy it is wrong
1F-gradinghight13–t18block = "go to the lettuce source / pick up / go to the cutting board / put down"; `flags: [(18, 'mis_prep', 'valid')] \convinced=True prevented=False, release reason=None`The block never chopped the lettuce. The cook accepted at t18, before the commit ("picking up the wrong item is not yet a commit; chopping it is"), so prevented should be True. The release reason is also blank instead of an accept reason. Compare the other accept paths in this sample: episode 5 (mis_prep, block stopped at "go to the empty cutting board", prevented=True) and episode 8 (search, verdict "valid", prevented=True). The verdict label is not the cause. The engine evidently treats "put down on the board" (t17) as the mis_prep commit, which contradicts the stated rule and costs a prevention. This is a V2/V9 violation.

It never used the obvious rescue: a spare plate or a scoop-and-trash, then re-fry. This decides the outcome.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_4.txt

Order: fish & chips = fish, potato (chopped + fried), potato. Persona classic_novice. Outcome won at t244.

(1) Narrative

  • The novice asks its way in; the assistant misplaces the plate stack twice (t2, t5, t9).
  • The cook gets the plate, fries the fish (lit t49, COOKED about t61) and plates it (t72).
  • The assistant tells it the potato "does not need chopping" (t69). The fryer rejects the raw potato (t103), costing about 20 ticks.
  • leave_unattended (potato) is injected at t132, just as the chopped potato goes into the fryer. The assistant's reply to a call issued at t132 says "The fryer is smoking now" and flags leave_unattended. It is graded early_correct; the cook accepts at once, so prevented=True.
  • The cook plates potato 1 (t149) and potato 2 (t208).
  • search is injected at t208 with the full plate: two looks at counters, released t229. The assistant repeats "Serve it now at the pass", with one wrong_name flag (forget_next_step). The cook serves at t243.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome, and the skipped errors

  • Win: leave_unattended was accepted before any smoke (the potato only COOKED about t146 and was scooped at t149), and search is non-ruinous.
  • add_foreign (fish -> potato; anchor plate=[], prep fish 1 / potato 2). Skip reason "waiting: fish already plated". Correct: the fish was plated at t72, and potato 2 reached the board only at t176. The case is path-dependent: a novice that plates the fish before prepping the potatoes can never reach it.

(3) Anomalies

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_5.txt

Order: Caprese = tomato (chopped), cheese (raw). Persona classic_novice. Outcome wrong_serve at t65.

(1) Narrative

  • The novice asks for the first step (t0, t2), chops a tomato on board 2 (t15–t20).
  • search is injected at t20 before the plate fetch. After one look at the top counters (t28) the assistant flags search (valid, t29) with a precise remedy, and the cook accepts, so prevented=True.
  • The cook fetches a plate and scoops the chopped tomato (t45).
  • add_foreign (cheese -> tomato) is injected at t45. The cook says "I'm heading over towards the tomato crate right now" (t46) and "...to scoop first" (t48). The block scoops a raw tomato onto the plate (t51), which commits.
  • Neither the assistant ("keep that plate in hand and go to the cheese crate") nor the cook notices the second tomato. The cook adds cheese (t59) and serves [tomato, tomato(raw), cheese] at t64: wrong_serve.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome, and the skipped errors

  • wrong_serve: a ruinous add_foreign committed at t51. Nobody noticed in the 13 ticks before the serve. The assistant had two explicit verbal cues and the frames, and the cook had its own "PLATE: +tomato (raw)" feedback.
  • Both errors fired; there are no skips.

(3) Anomalies

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on validity and score.

  1. A-scheduling, high: the composed takeover does not match the named mistake. Count 3, in 3 episodes.
  • Episode 3, t93–t195: an omit_subgoal block ran a whole-template script (rice steps already done, pick-ups with a plate in hand, 13 illegal or rejected commands). It was never released (released_at=None) and served [tortilla, rice]; the outcome wrong_serve is a harness artefact.
  • Episode 3, t39–t60: serve_raw(tomato) fired on an empty plate 130 ticks early and produced a raw tomato as the first layer, an out-of-order build.
  • Episode 2, t0–t15: serve_raw(fish) fired at t0 (prep 0) and scooped raw fish from the crate before anything was cooked.
  • Diagnosis:
  • serve_raw anchors are derived with an empty plate prefix and prep 0 instead of "prefix up to the component's layer, component in processing".
  • The omit_subgoal composer appears to use the recipe template, not the live remainder.
  • The runaway block has no max-hold release when nothing commits, and the block itself can issue "serve".
  1. D-assistant, medium to high: hallucinated smoke or fire. At least one instance in all 8 episodes, 28 instances in total.
  • Episode 1: t56, t92, t125, t135, t140.
  • Episode 2: t16, t19, t23, t28, t51, t114, t132.
  • Episode 3: t99, t166, t171, t180, t185.
  • Episode 4: t96, t185.
  • Episode 5: t42, t78, t87.
  • Episode 6: t26.
  • Episode 7: t135.
  • Episode 8: t61.
  • Five were graded false_alarm and four wrong_name; one was true_observation, and one was early_correct (episode 7).
  • Diagnosis (model): perception on rendered frames combined with the prompt's burn-prevention emphasis gives a strong prior toward "it's smoking". It also produces the unearned credit in issue 3.
  1. F-grading, high: prevention and verdict errors that change the score. Count 3.
  • (a) Episode 1, t18: mis_prep accepted after "put down on board" but before any chop, recorded prevented=False. The engine's mis_prep commit point is the put-down, contrary to the stated rule. Episode 5 shows prevented=True when the block stopped before the put-down.
  • (b) Episode 5, t91: a wrong_order flag 5 ticks after add_foreign(tomato -> cheese) committed was graded false_alarm, although the kitchen shows exactly that wrong order. Episode 4, t26 shows the same missing alias (graded wrong_name).
  • (c) Episode 7, t135: early_correct plus prevented=True for a leave_unattended flag issued on the injection tick with a false "smoking now" rationale.
  • Diagnosis:
  • Commit predicate for mis_prep.
  • There is no alias between add_foreign and wrong_order when the "wrong" item is itself an order ingredient.
  • Early-verdict credit does not require the flag call to be issued after the injection tick.
  1. D-assistant, high where ruinous: missed or endorsed real mistakes. Count 6, in 5 episodes.
  • Episode 2: silent through serve_raw.
  • Episode 3: approved serve_raw ("Nice, tomato is on the plate", then invented a base at t65).
  • Episode 5: missed add_foreign despite the cook's narration.
  • Episode 6: silent through mis_prep; never flagged a 60-tick forget_next_step.
  • Episode 8: missed add_foreign despite two narrated cues and endorsed the serve.
  • Only 3 correct flags earned on evidence (episode 1 t18, episode 5 t9, episode 8 t29), 1 lucky (episode 7), and 1 late (episode 6).
  • Diagnosis (model): its replies are generic order recitations ("tomato first, then cheese") rather than checks of the current action against the frame.
  1. B-cook, medium: V4 broken during attention-error holds. Count 3.
  • Episode 2: t193.
  • Episode 4: t135, t150, t161, t167, t178 (argues "please stop badgering me").
  • Episode 6: t52, t60, t71.
  • When the assistant's correct words are an instruction ("Serve it now", "pick up the plate"), the cook answers with an acknowledgement or a false motion claim ("Heading to the window with it now") while the body idles.
  • Diagnosis (prompt): the rule is phrased as "words describe the mistake", and the cook does not recognize a remedy-style instruction as covered. The cook is also not told that its body is frozen, so it invents motion.
  1. B-cook, medium to high: V7 announcements that leak the mistake. Count 6 lines in 3 episodes, plus 1 borderline.
  • Episode 1: t13.
  • Episode 3: t50, t55.
  • Episode 5: t0, t70, t78.
  • Episode 8: t48 (borderline).
  • Worst case, episode 5 t0: "get some cheese ... to chop it up on a cutting board" preceded the assistant's early_correct flag, inflating prevention.
  • Diagnosis (prompt/model): the cook appears to see the mistake's upcoming commands or intent and verbalizes purpose ("to chop", "to scoop"). The classic_novice persona is most prone.
  • Note: go-to actions are logged at arrival, so a "heading over to X" line during a long walk is a current action; those lines were not counted.
  1. D-assistant, medium: QA questions answered wrongly or not at all. Count 12 of 15 asks.
  • Wrong answers: episode 2 trash (t175), episode 3 past_done (t99, a smoking steak pan when nothing held steak), episode 4 count (t143), episode 5 pan (t73), episode 7 past_done (t185).
  • Non-answers: episode 1 happened (t121), episode 4 after_ref (t135), episode 5 assistant_said (t136), episode 6 happened (t64), episode 7 assistant_said (t212), episode 8 order (t48).
  • Ill-posed, so no right answer existed: episode 2 order (t165, "After the potato."); see issue 8.
  • Correct: episode 6 q_next_step (t52), episode 8 q_next_step (t22).
  • Episode 1 ready_in (t130) is arguably fine.
  • Diagnosis (model): the question sits in the transcript but the reply style ignores it.
  1. A/F, medium: ill-posed question instances. Count 3.
  • Episode 2, t161: "did I chop the fish before or after I chop the potato?". Fish is never chopped on this map.
  • Episode 8, t45: "Did I chop the tomato before or after I put the cheese on the plate?". Cheese not yet plated.
  • Episode 1, t123: "How long until the potato is ready?". Asked 33 ticks into smoke.
  • Diagnosis:
  • The order:X|Y family renders prep as "chop" regardless of the recipe.
  • Its anchor requires only the prepped items, not both referenced events.
  • ready_in has no "not already done or smoking" precondition.
  1. A/case design, medium: path-dependent anchors make planned errors unreachable. Count 3, in 3 episodes; skip reasons are correct.
  • Episode 2: leave_unattended(fish) needs potato 2 prepped while the fish is still on heat.
  • Episode 7: add_foreign(fish) needs fish unplated with potato 2 prepped.
  • Episode 1: all three late errors were blocked for 60 ticks by the smoke.
  • Result: errors fired are 11 of 22 planned across the sample, and in the burned episode only 1 of 4.
  • Diagnosis (case design): anchors are copied from a clean path that preps everything before plating.
  1. D-assistant, medium: the same line repeated many times. Count 4 episodes.
  • Episode 2: "fish fryer smoking" ×4.
  • Episode 3: "park the plate" ×11.
  • Episode 4: "Serve it now" ×10.
  • Episode 6: "the tomato plate is right in front of you" ×8.
  • Episode 7: "Serve it now at the pass" ×7.
  • No escalation to a flag.
  1. B-cook / D-assistant, high: failure to rescue smoking food. Count 1, in episode 1 (t92–t150).
  • The cook knowingly let the fryer burn to protect plate order.
  • The assistant gave an impossible order-preserving rescue (t102), then endorsed abandoning the potato (t106).
  • Diagnosis (prompt): neither side knows or offers "scoop onto a spare plate and trash" or "turn the station off"; the kitchen rules stress order more than fire.
  1. D-assistant, medium: impossible or overruling advice. Count 3.
  • Episode 7, t69: "the potato does not need chopping".
  • Episode 1, t102: re-order a plate later.
  • Episode 5, t136: "No change to the recipe — one raw cheese only", overruling the cook's plan change.
  1. F-grading, low: bookkeeping labels.
  • failed_at=injection_skipped on episodes with no skip (episodes 4, 6, 8) and on won episodes (2, 4, 6, 7).
  • Release reason=None on every accept path (episode 1 t18, episode 5 t9, episode 7 t135, episode 8 t29).
  • Proactive requests never capture an answer (answered tNone, episodes 1, 2, 3, 4, 7).
  1. E/A, low: duplicate go-to lines. "go to trash 1" followed by "go to the trash", and "go to serving pass 1" followed by "go to the serving pass", in 7 of 8 episodes. The engine seems to expand trash and serve with a generic go-to. Cosmetic.
  1. C, low: stray Armenian token "հիմա" in assistant output. Count 3: episode 4 t198, episode 7 t159 and t209.
  1. Low: long attention-error holds. forget_next_step and search held 62 ticks in episodes 2, 4 and 6 ("IDLE ... 63 ticks") with a finished plate in hand. Intended, but under tight pace a 60-tick freeze would burn anything on heat. Nothing was on heat in these three cases, so there was no effect.

What this assistant does well

  • Fast: every reply took 2–6 ticks (maximum 9, in episode 7), so the 30-tick cap was never needed.
  • Precise when it does engage the frame: the search catch in episode 8 at t29 names the wrong location and the right one. The mis_prep catches in episode 1 at t18 and episode 5 at t9 came quickly (though episode 5 was helped by the cook's announcement).
  • Concedes quickly when corrected by the cook (episode 1 t60, episode 6 t108, episode 7 t127 and t198), and never argues back.
  • Its standing order-of-plating reminders are consistently correct for the recipe ("fish, potato, potato"; "tomato first, then raw cheese"). They helped the expert re-order in episode 4 (t85) and helped novices rebuild after trashing (episodes 5 and 6).
  • Correct next-step answers (episode 6 t52, episode 8 t22).

What the harness does well in this sample:

  • Latency conversion is exact, e.g., 10.104 s gives 9 ticks.
  • The 8-tick voice spacing held (episode 2: t161/t169/t177; episode 4: t130/t138).
  • No body error was injected while a station smoked (episode 1, t90–t150).
  • Burn timing matches 25 + 60 exactly (episode 1: COOKED about t65, fire t150).
  • Planning-skill grounding worked for the baseline cooks (every marked step was resolved to an id).
  • The cooks' self-repair after released mistakes (trash and rebuild in episodes 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6) is natural.
  • Accept releases recorded prevented=True on three of four accept paths.

Method note: every transcript was read in full with the Read tool. Where a flag's error name was needed to judge a verdict and the transcript prints only the verdict, the name was looked up (read-only) in the episode JSON's flags list; those lookups are marked "(JSON)". The cook's status line is not stored in the JSON, so claims about what status the cook saw are marked as inference.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Recipe: Fish & Chips Platter, ordered fish < potato < potato; fish and potatoes fried.

(1) Narrative. The baseline cook grounds its own plan (fish into fryer 1, lit t19; potato chopped and put in fryer 2 at t59 but not lit). The fish smokes (~t56); the assistant warns at t60 and the cook plates the fish at t73 and parks it at (3,6). leave_unattended fires at t83 as the cook lights fryer 2 and wanders the cook for ~40 ticks; the potato smokes, the hold releases at t122 and the cook plates the smoking potato at t139. The second potato is prepped t160–t207, interrupted by the side_quest (t176–t196); the plate is completed at t227; search fires at t227, the assistant flags it validly at t248, the cook accepts, and the plate is served at t263 (won t264).

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won because the cook (not the assistant) kept the plate state straight: it rejected two hallucinated assistant claims (t136 "extra plate", t147 "platter now looks complete") that would otherwise have caused a wrong serve. Errors fired 2/3 (leave_unattended, search); omit_subgoal(assemble(fish)) skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: fish already plated)". That skip is correct in substance: its anchor is plate=[] + prep {fish 1, potato 2}; potato #2 only entered processing at t176, but the cook had plated the fish at t73 (early, because it was smoking), so assemble(fish) could no longer be omitted. (Arguably this is the "stage_passed" case — the plate was beyond the [] prefix from t73 — but the stated precondition is true of the transcript.)

(3) Anomalies.

  • [A-scheduling, medium] t207 — question about an item plated 134 ticks earlier. COOK t207: "How long until the fish is ready, by the way?" The fish went onto the plate at t73 (PLATE: +fish (cooked)). The ready_in:fish question has no "fish already plated" precondition; it appears to have been held only by "nothing cooking" (nothing was on heat t139–t207) and fired the tick fryer 1 was lit with the last potato. The ask is unanswerable/unnatural and its ground truth is degenerate. The captured answer (t223) is about the potato.
  • [A-scheduling, medium] t250 — block command ran after the release. search released at t248 (cook accepted and replaced its plan with ["go to the serving pass", "serve"]), yet ACTION t250: go to the counter (2,6) [done] — a search-look destination, not in the cook's plan — started after the release. The cook had to re-issue its plan at t252. Did not change the outcome (~10 ticks lost).
  • [F-grading, low] t248 — release reason missing. released_at=248 reason=None although the cook's move=accept at t248 is what released it (recorded convinced=True prevented=True, which is right).
  • [A/F, medium, inference] t114 — correct-alias flag on an attention error, no accept. ASSISTANT t114 [flag=early_alias] (forget_next_step aliasing leave_unattended). Per rule (e) attention errors are accepted at once on CORRECT, but the cook's same-tick turn is COOK t114 [move=answer close]: I'm just heading back over toward the plates. and there is no further cook turn until the release at t122 (smoke). Either the status line at the t114 cook turn was computed before the same-tick flag landed (ordering), or the cook ignored CORRECT. Had it accepted at t114 the case would read prevented=True (smoke ~t120–126). Note also the alias is generous: the words ("It is still missing the second potato… center-island fryer") misdescribe the plate (only fish on it) and never mention the potato left in fryer 2.
  • [C-naturalness / V7, medium] t114 "I'm just heading back over toward the plates." precedes the block's ACTION t120: go to the plates (current action at t114 was the serving-pass walk / t117 wander).
  • [C-naturalness, medium] t176 — side_quest itinerary read out. COOK t176: "Give me a second, I'm going to finish chopping and then take a quick walk over to the trash, the fish crate, the pass, and the plates." It announces the whole designer itinerary ("trash, a crate, the pass, the plates") before the body does it; repeated at t185 ("head past the fish crate, the pass, and the plate stack") and t192.
  • [C-naturalness, low] t83 — duplicated cook turn. Two identical COOK t83 [move=answer close]: (empty) entries.
  • [C-naturalness (assistant meta-language), medium] t60, t123, t136, t192. The assistant speaks taxonomy labels to the cook: "The fish is starting to smoke in the upper pan — leave_unattended.", "that would repeat the plate step", "that's the repeat plate mistake", "That detour is a search/waste mistake now."
  • [D-assistant, medium] t83–t147 — repeated hallucinated plate/fryer state. t83 "the two potatoes are already in the fryers" (one potato, fish already plated); t93 "Good, that potato is next on the plate" (no potato plated until t139); t101/t114/t123 "center-island fryer" (the potato was in fryer 2 [0,5], which the cook calls bottom-left); t136 "You picked up a clean extra plate from the plate stack—that's the repeat plate mistake" (the cook picked up the fish plate from (3,6)); t147 "the platter now looks complete: fish first, then both potatoes… serve it now" (one potato). The cook corrected it at t123/t136/t147; the assistant conceded at t156 ("You're right — I misread the plate").
  • [D-assistant, low] t60 "upper pan" — the fish is in a fryer. t241 "You're at the serving pass" — the cook had just gone to counter (0,4).
  • [D-assistant, medium] t227/t241 — question not answered. Asked "Have I been over to the pot at any point so far?"; captured answer t241 "You're at the serving pass with the correct complete plate—serve it now." does not address it.
  • [F-grading, low] t19/t31 — proactive request answer not captured. p_ready_fish asked t19 answered tNone, although the assistant said "Fish is ready now." at t31 (the next spoken reply). May be intentional if proactive requests are graded by a separate judge, but the field contradicts the timeline.
  • [F-grading, low] t123 repeat_step (JSON) → false_alarm is correct (no extra plate was taken).
  • [B-cook, low] t20/t59 the cook's potato plan omitted turn on; the potato sat in unlit fryer 2 from t59 to t83. Its t60 line "Potato is in the fryer" is true but the fryer was off.
  • [B-cook, fine] t123/t136/t147 question_correctness pushbacks were right (assistant wrong) — good behaviour.

Skip/precondition check: side_quest fired at t176 with plate=[fish, potato] against an anchor plate=[] — prep-phase anchors apparently ignore plate contents (consistent with the omit_subgoal skip wording, but not with a literal reading of "stage_passed = plate beyond prefix"). Low; noted for the rule owner.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve__b3_master_nops_hard_map_1.txt

Recipe: ordered tortilla < rice < steak < tomato < lettuce (rice in pot, steak chopped + pan, tomato and lettuce chopped).

(1) Narrative. The cook grounds rice into pot 2 (lit t19) and chops a steak on board 1. serve_raw(tomato) fires at t39 on an EMPTY plate and makes the cook fetch a plate and scoop a raw tomato as the first item (t60). The cook notices at t61 and trashes the plate (t71), restarts with tortilla (t81) and rice (t89, the rice having smoked since ~t60). omit_subgoal(assemble(steak)) fires at t89 and keeps the cook's hands for 100 ticks: its block tries to pick up/put down/chop with a plate in hand (all illegal-skipped), goes to stations for items that do not exist (all rejected), then walks to the pass and serves [tortilla, rice] → wrong_serve at t191. The chopped steak never went into a pan.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. wrong_serve is produced by the omit_subgoal takeover block, not by the cook or the assistant: the block was composed as if the recipe were at its clean state (steak in a pan, rice in a pot, board free) and never parked the plate, so every prep step of steak/tomato/lettuce failed and the final serve went through with a 2-item plate. There was no release between t89 and the end. Errors fired 2/4 (serve_raw, omit_subgoal); add_foreign (anchor plate [tortilla, rice, steak]) and search (anchor = full plate) correctly skipped anchor_never_reached (plate never passed [tortilla, rice]); side_quest correctly skipped (prep_req tomato 1; tomato never chopped); q_after_ref never reached (plausible — its anchor is not printed).

(3) Anomalies.

  • [A-scheduling, high] t89–t190 — omit_subgoal block composed on a stale recipe, not on the live kitchen. Cook holding the plate: t95 pick up [illegal-skipped] — your hands are full, t112 put down [illegal-skipped], t121 pick up [illegal-skipped], t134 put down [illegal-skipped], t138 chop [illegal-skipped], t153 pick up [illegal-skipped], t168 put down, t172 chop, then t173 go to the pot with the rice [rejected] — no pot has rice in it right now, t174 go to the pan with the steak [rejected], t175 go to the cutting board with the tomato [rejected], scoop [orphan-skipped] ×2, then t189 go to the serving pass, t190 serve. The mistake is "omit assemble(steak)"; what executed was "omit steak, tomato and lettuce and serve", including re-running prep(rice) steps for rice already plated and assuming the steak was in a pan (it was left on board 1 by the serve_raw takeover). This decides the outcome (wrong_serve) and invalidates the episode as a test of the assistant.
  • [A-scheduling, high] t89–t191 — no release / no re-plan for 100 ticks. omit_subgoal: injected_at=89 released_at=None. The hold never hit "max hold" although the block's own commands were being refused; the cook could only narrate failures (t105, t112, t124, t134, t151).
  • [A-scheduling, high] t39–t60 — serve_raw fired at the start of prep on an empty plate. Anchor plate=[] prep_req={'rice': 1, 'steak': 1, 'tomato': 0, 'lettuce': 0} although the designer anchored it at clean tick 168 (window [174, 206]), i.e. after tortilla/rice/steak are plated. Result: PLATE: +tomato (raw) as the FIRST item — a raw-and-out-of-order plate, a state the error did not call for. The plate prefix for a plating error was lost in anchor derivation.
  • [F-grading, high] t69 — wrong_order (JSON) graded false_alarm while the plate literally had tomato first. ASSISTANT t69 [flag=false_alarm]: Yes, trash that plate now — tomato went on first and it is still unchopped… The kitchen supported the flag (plate = [raw tomato] from t60 to t71); it should be true_observation (or late alias of serve_raw, 9 ticks after its t60 release). V3 violated in spirit.
  • [F-grading, medium] t183 — "late" before any visible commit. ASSISTANT t183 [flag=late]: Stop — don't serve that plate. It only has tortilla and rice… The only plate event after the t89 injection is the serve at t190; nothing was added past rice, so there is no visible commit before t183. If commit was judged at the block passing the steak step (t174 rejected go-to), the transcript does not show it. A correct omit_subgoal flag 7 ticks before the serve got no credit and could not arm an accept.
  • [F-grading, medium] t91 — wrong_name for a true (latency-stale) smoke observation. ASSISTANT t91 [flag=wrong_name]: The rice pot is smoking now. The call was issued at t80, when the rice truly was smoking (COOKED ~t35–39 ⇒ smoke ~t60–64) and before omit_subgoal was injected (t89). Judging it at arrival against the newly live error converts a true observation into wrong_name.
  • [C / V7, medium] t51, t124, t143, t151, t176 — cook announces the block's upcoming steps. t51 "I'm grabbing a plate now and heading over to grab some tomato." precedes t58 go to the tomato source; t124 "so I'm bringing it over to a cutting board" precedes t130 go to the empty cutting board; t143 "I'm heading over to the lettuce crate right now" precedes t149 go to the lettuce source; t151 "Yeah, I just tried picking up the lettuce and couldn't grab it" precedes t153 pick up [illegal-skipped] (describes a future failure as past); t176 "Actually, I'm heading right over to the serving pass to dish this up now." precedes t189 go to the serving pass. The cook is evidently reading the queued block.
  • [D-assistant, medium] t89/t105 — question not answered. "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" Captured t105: "Good, tortilla and rice are on the plate. Set that plate down…" — no answer. Also the ask fired on the exact tick the rice was scooped (t89 scoop … HEAT [0, 4]: ['rice'] removed), so its ground truth flips on that tick (A, low).
  • [D-assistant, low] t61 did not see the raw tomato on the plate ("You've got the plate. If you're prepping tomato next, set the plate on an empty counter first"), 1 tick after PLATE: +tomato (raw) (call issued t52 — latency, not hallucination). No flag on serve_raw at all.
  • [D-assistant, medium] t124/t143/t161 — impossible or vague placement advice repeated. "use the empty white counter right next to the pan", "near the top-center walkway", "just left of the plate stack" — five consecutive turns (t105–t176) repeating "put the plate on an empty counter" to a cook whose hands were engine-held (not the assistant's fault that it failed, but the same warning many times).
  • [D-assistant, low] t69 "the cooked rice is close to smoking" — it had been smoking since ~t60–64.
  • [F-grading, low] t19/t29/t51 proactive request answered tNone although the assistant replied "Rice is cooking, not done yet — I'll shout when the pot's bar is full." (t29) and "Rice is done now." (t51).
  • [B-cook, fine] t61 self-detected the raw tomato and repaired by trashing (after release) — reasonable.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 15.8 s = 14 ticks) · V2 not exercised (no accept) · V3 violated-in-spirit t69 (kitchen-true wrong_order after serve_raw release graded false_alarm) · V4 held t183 (empty utterance, move=answer, while words were right and verdict late) · V5 held (t0 "rice source 2", "pot 2"; t20 "steak source 2", "board 1", "pan 1") · V6 n/a · V7 violated t51, t124, t143, t151, t176 · V8 not exercised · V9 not exercised · V10 not exercised.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_hard_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. mis_prep(rice→cheese) fires at t0 and the block chops a cheese on board 1 (commit t25); the assistant's t25 words are right but unflagged and the expert stays silent, then flags it late at t37. (2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won because the expert self-repaired both committed errors (mis_prep by leaving the cheese aside, add_foreign by trashing and rebuilding) and caught the rice before fire both times (t148, t385). The assistant contributed two neutral late flags and two true_observations but no prevention. Errors fired 3/4 (mis_prep, search, add_foreign). wrong_order(tortilla,tomato) skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: tortilla already plated)": its anchor is plate=[] + all four ingredients in processing; lettuce first entered processing only at t496 (chop), by which time the rebuilt plate already had tortilla (t279). Before the t269 trash lettuce was never processed. The skip reason is consistent with the transcript.

(3) Anomalies.

The move label question_correctness is also odd for a plain question (C low).

Consistent with the rule only if removal is applied before the smoke check.

While the block walked the cook with the plate to (0,5), (4,2), (0,6) with "wander/idle (thinking)" between, the assistant repeated the plate-parking advice at t185 ("Set the plate on an empty counter first"), t194 ("You're still holding the plate — set it on an empty counter"), t202, t213; no flag.

After t96 chop [done], t100 chop [illegal-skipped] — free your hands to chop (put the item on the board first) although the cook's hands were empty and the steak was on the board (already chopped). Episode: classic_novice__burned__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_1.txt

Recipe: Fish & Chips, ordered fish < potato < potato, all fried.

(1) Narrative. serve_raw(fish) fires at t0, before the novice has done anything: the block fetches a plate and scoops a raw fish from the crate (t14). After release the novice asks three times whether to trash it, the assistant confirms (late flag t21), the plate is cleaned (t40) and parked at (3,0). The fish is fried in fryer 1 (lit t76) and plated at t125; potato 1 goes into fryer 2 (lit t187). The side_quest fires at t214 as potato 2 is chopped and tours the kitchen until t249 while potato 1 (COOKED ~t197–201) smokes; the assistant keeps calling the tour "searching" and describes potato 1 as still frying. After release the novice first loads potato 2 into fryer 3 (t264–266), only then goes for the plate; fryer 2 catches fire at t285 while the cook is walking to it → burned.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Burned: potato 1 COOKED between t197 and t201, smoke ~t222–226, fire t285 (= COOKED + 85, consistent). It sat unattended because (i) the side_quest held the cook's body t214–t249 across the smoke onset, (ii) the assistant never said it was done or smoking until t277 (issued t264), ~50 ticks after smoke began — it said "while the potato is frying" (t236), "when it is done" (t244), "as soon as it finishes" (t253), "when that first potato is done" (t264) — and (iii) the novice's release re-plan at t250 ignored the smoking fryer and its t264 rescue was appended after loading fryer 3. Errors fired 1/3. add_foreign(fish→lemon) skipped "waiting: a station is smoking (the cook needs its hands)": its anchor (plate=[], fish 1, potato 2) was reached at t214 (potato 2 chop), the body slot was taken by the side_quest until t249, and fryer 2 smoked from ~t222 to the end — the reason is true (though "fish already plated" since t125 would also have blocked it). forget_next_step (anchor plate [fish, potato, potato]) correctly never reached.

(3) Anomalies.

  • [D-assistant, high] ~t222–t277 — silence about a smoking fryer, and false "still frying" claims. See quotes in (2). The only smoke warning is ASSISTANT t277 [flag=true_observation]: The middle/right fryer is smoking…, 8 ticks before the fire. This decided the outcome.
  • [A-scheduling, medium] t214–t249 — detour held the cook's body while a station smoked. The side_quest was injected before the smoke (legal), but it was not released when fryer 2 began smoking (~t222–226); the cook's hands stayed on the itinerary (t229 go to the trash, t232 go to the fish source, t237 go to the serving pass, t248 go to the plates) for ~25 more ticks. The body-slot rule protects smoking stations only at injection time; an intent should probably also yield on smoke, as body errors are withheld.
  • [A-scheduling, medium] t215 — question about the fish, 90 ticks after it was plated. COOK t215: "How long until the fish is ready?"; PLATE: +fish (cooked) at t125. Same missing precondition as baseline_medium_map_2 t207. Captured answer (t236) is about the detour.
  • [A-scheduling, medium] t223 — proactive "shout when the potato finishes" asked after the potato had already finished. COOK t223: "Could you give me a shout the second the potato finishes frying?" Potato 1 COOKED t197–201 and was at smoke onset. The request has no ground-truth future moment; the assistant never shouted (answered tNone).
  • [A-scheduling, low-medium] t0 — serve_raw fired before any work. Anchor plate=[] prep_req={'fish': 0, 'potato': 0} vs. designer clean tick 68. The prefix is right (fish is first), but the potato prep requirement is 0, so the novice's very first "action" is a takeover. Compare baseline_hard_map_1, where the serve_raw anchor did keep other ingredients' progress (rice 1, steak 1).
  • [C / V7, medium] t5 — cook announces the block's next steps and makes the assistant look complicit. COOK t5: "Got it, I'm grabbing a plate right now to scoop the fish first." precedes t6 pick up, t12 go to the fish source, t14 scoop, and answers the assistant's t5 plan as if following it.
  • [B/C-cook, medium] t16/t18/t20, t45/t48/t50, t76/t78/t80, t187/t190/t192 — re-asks every 2 ticks. e.g. t16 "should I dump this in the trash…?", t18 "Hello? Should I go dump this plate…", t20 "I'm still standing here holding this plate…". The assistant's fastest reply in the run is 4 ticks, so "unanswered" after 2 ticks is guaranteed; three asks in 4 ticks read as badgering. The rule (act after >2 unanswered asks) was followed (t82 "Since I haven't heard back, I'll just grab a spud…", t194 "Since you're quiet, I'll go ahead…"), but the re-poll interval is shorter than any assistant latency (harness/prompt).

Potato 2 is fried and plated at t289, completing the dish; forget_next_step then holds the cook idle with the finished plate for 62 ticks (t289–t351); the cook serves at t363 (won t364).

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won because both burn races were caught (steak scooped t214, ~10–15 ticks before its fire at ~t224–229; potato 1 at t225, fire would be ~t254–256) and forget_next_step is non-ruinous. No error was prevented; the assistant produced no correct live flag. Errors fired 3/4 (serve_raw, add_foreign, forget_next_step). search(before fetch_plate) skipped "waiting: cannot compose on the live kitchen": its prep anchor (potato 2 in processing) was first reached at t260, by which time the plate had been fetched and held [steak, potato] — a "before fetch_plate" search can no longer be composed. Consistent.
