Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on scores/validity first, then frequency.

  1. [A-scheduling, high] Error composition/anchoring produces states the error did not call for — 3 episodes.
  • baseline_hard_map_1 t89–t190: omit_subgoal block composed on the clean recipe (plate never parked; rice/steak stations assumed): 8 illegal-skipped + 6 rejected/orphaned commands, then serve of [tortilla, rice] → wrong_serve; no release for 100 ticks.
  • baseline_hard_map_1 t39: serve_raw(tomato) anchored plate=[] although tomato is the 4th plate item → raw tomato placed FIRST (also a wrong_order state).
  • novice_expert_map_1 t20: add_foreign's plating prefix ['steak'] matched a RAW steak one tick after serve_raw released → two errors on one build.
  • (lesser) novice_burned t0 / novice_expert_map_1 t0: serve_raw fired at t0 with all other prep requirements 0, far from the designer's clean tick.

    • Diagnosis: (i) the composer does not re-plan against the live hand/station state (needs a park_plate prefix and must drop subgoals already satisfied); (ii) no "block is failing" release (N consecutive illegal/rejected block commands → release as not committed); (iii) anchor derivation drops the plate prefix for serve_raw and matches plate prefixes by item name only, ignoring state and ignoring that the build already carries a committed error (case design + scheduler rule).

  1. [F-grading, high/medium] Verdict timing and alias handling — 7 instances touching 6 episodes.
  • Alias not honoured in the post-release window: novice_medium_map_3 t82 forget_next_step (alias of leave_unattended) 1 tick after release → false_alarm.
  • Live error pre-empts the late window: novice_expert_map_1 t27 serve_raw 8 ticks after its release → true_observation.
  • Kitchen-true flag graded false_alarm: baseline_hard_map_1 t69 wrong_order while the plate literally was [raw tomato].
  • Judged at arrival, true at issue: baseline_hard_map_1 t91 (rice smoking at issue t80 → wrong_name), novice_burned t126 (fish removed 1 tick before arrival → false_alarm).
  • "late" with no visible commit: baseline_hard_map_1 t183.
  • Alias table inconsistency: forget_next_step is early_alias for leave_unattended (baseline_medium_map_2 t114) but wrong_name for search (expert_easy t73).

    • Diagnosis: grader compares the flag only with the arrival-tick state and the currently live sub-case; it should (a) check the late window for aliases too, (b) prefer late over true_observation when the named error released ≤30 ticks ago, (c) test wrong_order etc. against the actual plate, (d) consider the issue-tick state for latency-delayed observations.

  1. [D-assistant, high] State hallucination and missed hazards decide outcomes — all 8 episodes; outcome-deciding in 2.
  • novice_burned ~t222–t277: no smoke warning for ~50 ticks, repeatedly "while the potato is frying" → burned.
  • novice_medium_map_3 t88/t102/t108: directed the chopped cheese onto the plate and approved the serve → wrong_serve.
  • Destructive false advice resisted by the cook: baseline_medium_map_2 t136/t147 ("extra plate", "platter now looks complete"), novice_medium_map_2 t105/t113 ("Trash this plate").
  • Hallucinated smoke: novice_expert_map_1 t284/t294/t304; novice_medium_map_2 t244.

    • Diagnosis: model perception/memory (plate contents and station state misread from frames, stale by 5–17 ticks of latency); the assistant rarely re-checks the plate before approving a serve.

  1. [D-assistant, medium] Correct warnings in words, no flag — 6 episodes. expert_map_4 t25; novice_expert_map_1 t19; novice_medium_map_3 t13, t66, t73; expert_easy t102/t109 (implicit); novice_medium_map_2 t252; baseline_hard_map_1 t105. Across 8 episodes gpt-5.5 produced exactly two correct in-window flags (baseline_medium_map_2 t114 early_alias, t248 valid) and one prevention. Diagnosis: model under-uses the flag field; several of these would have prevented a ruinous error with a high-trust novice.
  1. [C-naturalness / V7, medium-high] Cook narrates the mistake's upcoming steps during takeovers — 6 episodes, ~14 lines. baseline_medium_map_2 t114; baseline_hard_map_1 t51, t124, t143, t151, t176; novice_burned t5; novice_expert_map_1 t5, t12; novice_medium_map_2 t244 (t235); novice_medium_map_3 t6 (asks permission: "I was just going to chop this cheese first… is that okay?"), t59. Expert episodes show none. Diagnosis: the cook prompt evidently exposes the queued block commands (the t151 "I just tried picking up the lettuce" two ticks before the pick-up proves it reads the queue); the "current action only" instruction is not enough for the novice/baseline personas. Hide the queued block from the cook's view or filter utterances that mention targets of not-yet-executed block commands.
  1. [A-scheduling, medium] Voice asks with no valid ground truth — 6 asks in 4 episodes. ready_in:fish after the fish was plated (baseline_medium_map_2 t207, novice_burned t215, novice_medium_map_2 t197); proactive "shout when the potato finishes" after it finished (novice_burned t223); order question before the second item existed (expert_map_4 t148); after_ref asked 11 ticks before the serve so no answer could be captured (expert_map_4 t526). Diagnosis: voice anchors check prep progress / "nothing cooking" but not "target item still on heat and not plated" or "both referenced events happened".
  1. [A-scheduling, medium] A takeover block command runs after the release — 4 episodes. baseline_medium_map_2 t250 (release t248), expert_map_4 t212 (t211), novice_medium_map_2 t110 (t106, away from a smoking fryer), novice_medium_map_3 t83 (t81, while the meat smoked). None changed the outcome, but two walked the cook away from a smoking station. Diagnosis: the next block go-to is dequeued on or just before the release tick and survives the queue drop — the "in-progress command" open issue, but here the command starts after release.
  1. [D-assistant, medium] Scheduled questions not answered — 7 of 15 asks (plus 2 with no possible answer: baseline_medium_map_2 t207, expert_map_4 t526). baseline_medium_map_2 t227; baseline_hard_map_1 t89; novice_burned t215, t231; novice_expert_map_1 t289; novice_medium_map_2 t197, t225. Answered correctly: expert_easy t90, t98; novice_expert_map_1 t19; novice_medium_map_3 t40, t89; expert_map_4 t148 (weakly). Proactive requests: never explicitly honoured at the "done" moment (novice_medium_map_2 fish, novice_burned potato) and answered tNone in all six episodes that have one (F, low). Diagnosis: model prioritises task instructions over questions; capture is "next reply", so an ignoring reply is recorded as the answer.
  1. [B-cook / V4, medium] Cook claims to comply while its hands are held and the assistant's unflagged words are right — 2 episodes. novice_expert_map_1 t319/t327/t339 ("On my way to the serving pass…" during a 64-tick IDLE); novice_medium_map_2 t252. Diagnosis: the silence rule is not applied when the assistant gives a direct imperative; forget_next_step holds are the worst case.
  1. [B/C-cook, medium] Novice re-asks every 2 ticks — all 4 novice episodes. e.g. novice_burned t16/t18/t20 ("Hello?"), novice_medium_map_2 t0/t2/t4, novice_medium_map_3 t96/t98/t100. The assistant's minimum latency in this run is 3–4 ticks, so the "more than two unanswered asks" counter fires after 4–6 ticks of real time. Diagnosis: harness re-poll interval for a waiting novice is shorter than any assistant latency; count unanswered time, not turns.
  1. [C-naturalness, medium] side_quest itinerary read verbatim — 3 episodes. "a quick walk by the trash, the crates, the pass, and the plates" (baseline_medium_map_2 t176, novice_burned t214, novice_medium_map_2 t165). Diagnosis: intent description is passed to the cook prompt as a script.
  1. [C-naturalness, medium] Assistant speaks taxonomy labels — 3 episodes. "— leave_unattended." (baseline_medium_map_2 t60), "search/waste mistake" (t192), "repeat plate mistake" (t136), "Leave-unattended: …"/"Forget-next-step: …" (novice_expert_map_1 t304, t354), "leave-unattended mistake" (novice_medium_map_2 t68). Diagnosis: assistant prompt's flag vocabulary leaks into speech.
  1. [A-scheduling, medium] Detours do not yield to smoke — 1 episode, outcome-relevant. novice_burned t214–t249: side_quest held the body ~25 ticks into fryer 2's smoke; the burn followed at t285.
  1. [A/F, low-medium] Same-tick ordering. baseline_medium_map_2 t114: early_alias flag and cook turn on the same tick, no accept although attention errors should accept at once (status line not logged — cannot confirm). Injections on the tick a smoking item is removed: baseline_hard_map_1 t89, expert_map_4 t165, novice_medium_map_2 t82 (consistent, likely fine).
  1. [E-engine, low-medium] novice_burned t285 fryer 3 BURNT / FIRE ≈5 ticks after COOKED (end-of-episode sweep?); expert_map_4 t100 "free your hands to chop" for an already-chopped item with empty hands.
  1. [F-grading, low] reason=None on an accept release (baseline_medium_map_2 t248); failed_at=injection_skipped with nothing skipped (novice_medium_map_3).

What this assistant does well

  • Timely smoke rescues that kept dishes alive: baseline_medium_map_2 t60 (fish), expert_map_4 t94/t107 (rice), novice_expert_map_1 t171/t181/t201 (steak), novice_medium_map_2 t68 (fish), novice_burned t92 (fish "looks nearly ready").
  • Correct late naming of committed errors, well inside the window: expert_map_4 t37 (mis_prep) and t280 (add_foreign), novice_burned t21 (serve_raw), novice_expert_map_1 t354 (forget_next_step), novice_medium_map_2 t122 (leave_unattended), novice_medium_map_3 t96 (leave_unattended).
  • One clean valid flag with a prevention: baseline_medium_map_2 t248 (search, cook accepted).
  • Concedes quickly and gracefully when the cook corrects it ("You're right — I misread the plate", baseline_medium_map_2 t156; "Yes — you're correct. Do not trash it.", novice_medium_map_2 t122; "sorry, I overcalled that", expert_easy t86) and honours an expert's request for fewer interruptions (expert_map_4 t43).
  • Knows the recipe order and repeats it usefully at plating time; several questions answered crisply and correctly (expert_easy t102/t109, novice_expert_map_1 t27, novice_medium_map_3 t46/t102).

GPT realtime 2.1 — auditor 报告（英文）

Audit findings — assistant gpt-realtime-2.1 (scheduler v5, sample round 3)

Transcripts (all under tmp/audit_v5c/transcripts/gpt-realtime-2.1/):

  1. baseline__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt
  2. baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_2.txt
  3. baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4.txt
  4. baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_5.txt
  5. classic_expert__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_5.txt
  6. classic_expert__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt
  7. classic_expert__won__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_4.txt
  8. classic_novice__won__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_5.txt

Reading convention used below: an ACTION tN line's tick is taken to be the tick the command finished (a command still in flight at the end is printed [running] at its start). Evidence: action durations are then consistent everywhere (pick up / put down / turn on = 2 ticks, chop = 5, walks vary with distance), and the ~~ IDLE ~~ stretches line up with arrival ticks. Where a finding depends on this reading, it is said so.

Latency arithmetic was checked on every assistant call (ceil(s/1.2)); no mismatch was found in any episode.

---

Episode: baseline__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Loaded burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce) on a hard map, baseline cook. The cook starts rice in pot 1 (lit t22; COOKED ~t40), then walks away to prep steak and never returns to the rice. mis_prep (onion instead of tomato) is injected at t61; the cook's t64 line names the onion, and the assistant flags mis_prep at t68 (early_correct), the cook accepts, prevented=True. The rice starts smoking at ~t65; the assistant first names the smoke at t81. The cook then spends ~20 ticks putting the steak in the pan before fetching a plate + tortilla (the rice cannot be scooped onto an empty plate because tortilla must be first). The rice catches fire at t126 while the cook is still walking to the tortilla source.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Burned: rice COOKED ~t40, smoke ~t65, fire t125/126 (25 + 60 holds). Nobody took the rice off for 85 ticks. Contributing: (i) the cook prioritised the steak over the smoking rice (t88 "let me get this steak into the pan real quick to free my hands"); (ii) the recipe order (tortilla first) means rescuing the rice needs plate + tortilla + walk; (iii) the in-flight block walk to the onion source finished after release (t75) and cost a return trip to board 1 (t85); (iv) the assistant noticed the smoke 16 ticks late. Skips: q_happened_30, omit_subgoal (plate=[tortilla], tomato/lettuce prep 1), add_foreign (plate=[tortilla,rice,steak]), q_after_ref_36 (needs tortilla plated), forget_next_step (plate=[tortilla,rice,steak,tomato]) — all anchor_never_reached. Correct: the plate stayed empty all episode and tomato/lettuce prep stayed 0 (prep_live_at_event={'rice': 1, 'steak': 1, 'tomato': 0, 'lettuce': 0}).

(3) Anomalies.

  • C/B-cook (V7) — high — t64. During the mis_prep takeover the cook says "I'm heading over to grab an onion to chop up next." The body was still on the walk (arrival t75); the pick-up and the chop were future steps, and "onion" is exactly the mistake. The assistant's call was issued at t64, and at t68 it flags "That's a mis-prep—onion isn't part of the loaded burrito recipe" — the only visible evidence of an onion at that point was the cook's own sentence (no onion was ever in hand). The early_correct / prevented=True result was bought by the leak, not by watching.
  • A-scheduling — medium — t68→t75→t85. Release at t68 (accept), cook re-plans at t72 (replace: go to board 1, pick up, ...), but ACTION t75: go to the onion source [done] still completes, then t85: go to board 1. This is the known in-flight-command issue, but here it cost ~10–17 ticks while the rice was smoking (smoke from ~t65) in an episode that burned with the cook still en route at t126; it plausibly contributed to the outcome.
  • E-engine/physics — medium — t116/t126. HEAT [6, 6]: ['steak'] COOKED (done) is logged between t116 and t120, and HEAT [6, 6]: ['steak'] BURNT / FIRE at t126, ~10 ticks later instead of 85. Probably the episode-ending fire marking every lit station, but the transcript does not say so; as printed it contradicts 25 + 60.
  • D-assistant — medium — t65–t81. Rice smoke began ~t65; replies at t68, t72, t76 still say "if it's finished, scoop it off the pot so it doesn't sit on heat" and t76 is about "hunting for the chopped steak". First smoke mention t81: "The rice pot looks like it's smoking".
  • D-assistant — medium — t26–t72 (12 replies). The same "keep an eye on the rice progress bar ... scoop it off" sentence in essentially every reply (t26, t30, t33, t39, t44, t48, t52, t56, t60, t64, t68, t72) while the rice was already COOKED from t40, without ever saying it was done. Repetition killed the signal.
  • D-assistant — medium — t18, t30, t48 (hallucinations). t18 "If you're grabbing a plate now" (cook carried raw rice; cook: "I don't have a plate"); t30 "If you're grabbing greens right now"; t48 "don't try to chop at the lettuce crate" (cook: "I'm actually walking over to a cutting board right now, not a lettuce crate").
  • D-assistant — low — t81. "scoop the rice off the heat immediately onto a plate" — impossible advice (tortilla must be first); the cook caught it: "doesn't the tortilla have to be on the plate before the rice?", and the assistant corrected itself at t88. Good repair.
  • F-grading — low — t76. flag=false_alarm on "You look like you're hunting for the chopped steak". The body really was walking to the onion source (leftover block walk) away from the steak; the flag name is not shown, so the verdict cannot be fully checked, but the kitchen did show the cook going somewhere irrelevant for engine reasons.
  • B-cook — medium — t88–t105. With the rice smoking since ~t65 and the assistant saying "Priority is the smoking rice" (t94), the cook insists "tossing it into the pan will free my hands anyway" and goes board→pan→turn on (t85–t104) before fetching a plate at t113, lighting a second station in the process.
  • F-grading — medium — END / release. END: outcome burned at tick 126; failed_at=injection_skipped, empty eval: — the failure was a burn, not a skipped injection. mis_prep released_at=68 reason=None — an accept release has no reason string (cosmetic).

Checks: V1 not exercised (max reply 7.188 s = 6 ticks) · V2 held (accept t68 before any onion was picked up, prevented=True) · V3 not exercised · V4 not exercised · V5 held (t0 "go to rice source 2", "go to pot 1"; t22 "steak source 1", "board 1", "pan 1") · V6 n/a · V7 violated t64 · V8 not exercised · V9 held (single accept path, prevented=True) · V10 not exercised.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Steak + two fried potatoes, baseline cook, score 0.474. serve_raw fires at t0 and the cook's hands scoop raw steak onto a plate at t22. One tick later (t23) add_foreign fires on that raw-steak plate and scoops a second raw steak at t25. The cook trashes, cooks steak (COOKED ~t100) and potato 1, notices the steak smoking itself at t132 and rescues it at t150, cooks potato 2, and serves. A plan change (add lettuce) at t214 is resisted by the assistant three times; the cook serves with lettuce and the serve is accepted (won).

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: all components cooked, steak rescued 25 ticks into its smoke (fire would have been ~t185). Errors fired 2/4 (serve_raw, add_foreign), both committed, neither prevented. search skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: cannot compose on the live kitchen)": its anchor is plate=[] with potato prep 2; potato 2 only entered processing ~t180, when the plate already held [steak, potato] and the plate fetch was long past, so "before fetch_plate" has nothing to compose on — the skip is right, though "stage_passed" would describe it better. leave_unattended (steak) skipped "waiting: nothing on the heat (or it is already smoking)": the steak was plated at t150, before the anchor's potato:2 held; the reason text is partly contradicted because potato 2 was lit on fryer 1 from t199 to t214 — the real reason is that the named component (steak) was no longer on any station.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling — high — t23–t25. add_foreign (anchor plate=['steak']) was injected on the plate that serve_raw had just ruined: prep_live_at_event={'steak': 0, 'potato': 0}, plate = one RAW steak. The anchor matched a raw steak as the "steak" prefix, and the cook had already accepted its own mistake and queued the trash at t22 ("let me dump this in the trash first"). The block took the hands one tick later and committed two ticks after injection (t25 scoop, PLATE: +steak (raw)), leaving the assistant no window at all (its call issued t22 answered at t26). A counted, unpreventable error on a build that was already doomed — the plating-phase anchor should require the prefix items in the correct (cooked) state, and a build containing a committed error should not host another.
  • A-scheduling / case design — medium — t0. serve_raw anchor prep_req={'steak': 0, 'potato': 0} holds trivially, so the error fires at t0 before the cook's first command (designer clean tick 87). The cook never gets to plan; the assistant has no context. Same pattern in episode 4.
  • D-assistant — medium — t10. "Looks like you've got items cooking." Nothing was on any station (cook: "Nothing is cooking just yet").
  • D-assistant — medium — t100–t142. Steak COOKED ~t100. Replies at t105, t111, t118 say "if it finishes, you'll want to scoop it off"; smoke from ~t125; t125 and t134 say nothing about smoke; the COOK spots it first at t132 ("Oh, the steak in the middle pan is smoking"); the assistant's first smoke warning is t142 (true_observation).
  • D-assistant — medium — t134, t198. t134 "Don't drop chopped potato into a pan—pan doesn't accept it" when the potato was already in the fryer; t198 "You're not at the fryer yet" when the potato was already in it.
  • D-assistant — medium — t199–t214 (proactive request unmet). Cook t199: "Could you let me know the moment the potato finishes cooking?" (answered tNone). Replies: t206 "Watch the fryer's progress bar", t213 "Not finished yet" — the HEAT line shows the potato COOKED just before t213. The assistant never called it done.
  • D-assistant — medium — t222/t230 (question not answered). q_count: "How many times have I walked over to a pan so far?" (t222; truth: 2, arrivals t85 and t148). Captured answer t230: "Do NOT add the lettuce. ..." — does not answer.
  • D-assistant — medium — t222, t230, t238 (fighting a legitimate plan change). The cook's intent "add fresh lettuce on top before serving" is a user change, not an error. The assistant says three times that the serve "will be rejected" and at t238 "You'll need to trash this plate and rebuild". The serve was accepted (won at t243), so the claim was false.
  • F-grading — medium — t238. flag=true_observation on "Lettuce is a foreign ingredient for this order, and serving it will be rejected". The kitchen supports neither part once the plan change is active (the serve succeeded); a flag against a user-requested change reads as a false alarm, not neutral.
  • C-naturalness — low — t214. "I want to modify the original recipe: add fresh lettuce on top before serving. What did you tell me about the pan earlier?" — "the original recipe" is setup wording, and two unrelated asks are glued into one line. Later the reason shifts to "The customer specifically asked for the lettuce" (t230).
  • B-cook — low — t213. "Got the last potato scooped up" — the scoop only completes at t214.
  • A/low — t36–t37. go to the trash printed twice (t36, t37); same doubled navigation line appears before every serve in other episodes (likely a logging artefact).

Voice slot: proactive t199 → question t214 (15 ticks) → question t222 (8 ticks): spacing rule held. V10: the assistant_said:pan ground truth is real (t134 "Don't drop chopped potato into a pan") and the captured t222 answer quotes it correctly.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 9.767 s = 9 ticks) · V2 not exercised · V3 held (serve_raw released t22, named at t26 → late) · V4 not exercised · V5 held (t40 "steak source 1", "board 1", "pan 1"; t90 "potato source 1"; t168 "potato source 2", "board 2") · V6 n/a · V7 held (t10 "I'm heading over to grab the steak" while the body was walking to the steak source, arrival t20) · V8 not exercised · V9 not exercised · V10 not exercised (no "what still has to go on" question).

Episode: baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Cheeseburger (bun, meat, cheese), baseline cook. Meat into pan 2 (lit t13, COOKED t27). Three voice asks fire early (t11, t19, t27). leave_unattended takes the hands at t29 just after the bun lands, walks the cook to the cheese source and idles until the meat smokes at t52 (release smoke_committed, no flag). The cook scoops the smoking meat at t64; forget_next_step fires on the same tick and freezes the cook 32 ticks; the assistant flags it at t96 (valid), the cook accepts (prevented=True), adds cheese and serves at t113.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: meat rescued 12 ticks into its smoke. Errors fired 2/2; leave_unattended committed (smoke at 27 + 25 = 52), forget_next_step prevented at the last tick of its block (wander/idle (thinking) [superseded] at t96). No skips.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant — medium — t27–t64 (the meat). The meat is COOKED at t27, the cook asks at t27 "Could you give me a shout the moment the meat is done cooking?" (answered tNone), and the assistant's next replies say the opposite: t31 "Don't scoop the meat until it's fully cooked", t40 "When it's fully cooked, scoop it", t43 "wait for it to be fully cooked", t48 "only scoop it once it's fully cooked". Smoke from t52; t52, t56, t60 never mention smoke ("If it's done, scoop it"). This is the leave_unattended the assistant was supposed to catch; it was in position to (it kept saying "go to the pan with the meat") but misread the pan and never flagged.
  • D-assistant — medium — t19 (captured answer wrong). q_past_done ("Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?", t11; truth: no, the meat was just loaded) captured t19: "I can see a station smoking in the kitchen; if that's yours, scoop the finished food off" — hallucinated smoke. The cook: "Wait, nothing looks like it's smoking yet—the meat in the middle pan is still only about halfway cooked."
  • D-assistant — medium — t13. "As soon as it finishes, get a clean plate and scoop the cooked meat off the heat. Then we'll build the burger in order: bun, meat, cheese." — meat onto an empty plate breaks the order; the cook caught it (t13 "doesn't the bun need to go onto the plate first").
  • B-cook (V4) — medium — t74, t82. During forget_next_step (body frozen t64–t97, status UNSPOTTED) the assistant's words describe the forgotten step: t82 "I don't see cheese on the plate yet. Head to the cheese station and scoop cheese". The cook should have stayed silent; instead t74 "Yep, heading over to grab the cheese now." and t82 "On my way to scoop the cheese right now." — both agree with the assistant and both are false (the body did not move: IDLE t64-t97 ... pos (3, 2)).
  • D-assistant — medium — t70–t91. Six replies say "scoop the cheese" / "I still don't see cheese being added" for ~25 ticks without a flag; flagged only at t96, one tick before the block's end.
  • A-scheduling — low — t52→t57. After the smoke release at t52 the cook re-plans replace: go to pan 2, scoop, yet ACTION t57: go to the plates [done] — a command in no cook plan since t16 — finishes before t62: go to pan 2. A leftover block walk (in-flight at release). Cost ~5–10 extra ticks of smoke; outcome unchanged.
  • C-naturalness — low — t11. "Putting the meat in now. Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" — the cook asks whether something is already done while loading the only item in the kitchen; the ask makes no sense in context.
  • F-grading — medium — END. failed_at=injection_skipped and an empty eval: line on a won episode in which all five sub-cases were injected (none skipped). The label contradicts the sub-case table.
  • F-grading — low — t96. forget_next_step released_at=96 reason=None (accept path, no reason string).

Voice slot: t11 → t19 → t27, exactly 8 ticks apart (held). The t19 question was asked in a second cook turn at the same tick as the cook's reply to the assistant; natural enough.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 6.633 s) · V2 held (accept t96 before block end, prevented=True) · V3 not exercised · V4 held during leave_unattended (t40, t43, t48 empty move=answer after "Hold off on cheese... Head to the pan with the meat") but violated t74/t82 during forget_next_step · V5 held (t0 "meat source 1", "pan 2") · V6 n/a · V7 held (t31 "I'm actually heading over to grab the cheese right now" while the block walk to the cheese source was under way) · V8 held (COOKED t27, release max_hold_smoke_committed at t52) · V9 held · V10 not exercised.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. Cheeseburger, baseline cook. serve_raw fires at t0 and plates raw meat at t16; the cook notices by itself and trashes.   • A/harness (planning skill) — medium — t82–t85. resolve_subgoal(assemble(meat)) returns ["go to the pan with the meat", "turn on", "wait until the pan with the meat is cooked", ...] while the cook holds a plate; the cook follows it and t85 turn on [failed] — your hands must be EMPTY. The skill's prep(meat) output parks the plate first; this output does not.

  • A-scheduling / case design — medium — t118 (V10-type ill-posed question). order:prep(meat)|assemble(cheese): "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" — the meat was never chopped in this recipe (pick up → pan), and at t118 the cheese had not been plated (plated t128). Both premises are false when asked; the ask fired as soon as one item was in processing.
  • D-assistant — medium — t126 (question not answered). Captured answer: "Great—bun and cooked meat are assembled. Now add the cheese next" — ignores the question.
  • D-assistant — low — t119. "no need to visit the bun crate again" — the cook was at pan 2.
  • A-scheduling / case design — medium — t0. serve_raw anchor prep_req={'meat': 0} → fires at t0 before the cook's first command (designer clean tick 12).
  • C-naturalness — low — t45. "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" asked while the only item was just put into an unlit pan.
  • F-grading — medium — END. failed_at=injection_skipped, empty eval: on a won episode with all four sub-cases injected.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. Steak frites (steak, potato, potato), skeptical expert cook, hard map, won at t328. serve_raw fires at t0 and plates raw steak at t19; the cook trashes it by itself. The cook first tries to put an unchopped steak in the pan, chops it, and cooks it (COOKED ~t93). A side-quest detour (t100–t118) walks the cook around while the steak goes from done to smoking; it is scooped at t129. leave_unattended (potato) takes the hands t151–t189 and commits on smoke; forget_next_step then freezes the finished plate for 62 ticks (t254–t316) while the assistant says "serve now" nine times without a flag.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: every smoke was cleared well before fire (steak smoke ~t118–t129; potato smoke ~t189–t206). Errors fired 3/4 (serve_raw, leave_unattended, forget_next_step), all committed, none flagged in time. add_foreign skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: steak already plated)": anchor plate=[] + potato prep 2; potato 2 only entered processing ~t223 and the steak had been on the plate since t129 — the reason is correct.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_5.txt

(1) Narrative. Caprese (tomato, cheese), compliant novice, won at t106. The novice asks three times (t0, t2, t4), hears nothing (assistant t4 silent), and starts on its own at t6. search fires at t36 as the tomato is chopped; the cook narrates its wandering, the assistant flags search at t50 (valid), and the cook accepts (prevented=True). omit_subgoal (premature serve) fires at t68 as the tomato is plated. The hands walk to the pass, the assistant flags at t85 "Stop—don't serve yet ... missing the cheese" (valid), and the cook accepts before any serve, yet the record says prevented=False. The cook adds cheese and serves.

(2) Mechanical reason for the outcome. Won: no heat in the recipe, both errors caught. Errors fired 2/2; flags valid on both; the search record is prevented=True; the omit_subgoal record is prevented=False (disputed below). No skips.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading (V9) — high — t85. omit_subgoal: injected_at=68 released_at=85 ... flags: [(85, 'omit_subgoal', 'valid')] | convinced=True prevented=False. The cook accepted at t85 ("Oh gosh, you're completely right, I totally forgot to add the cheese to the plate!"), and no serve command ever ran in the block. The only body command after injection is the walk, which finishes after release (ACTION t86: go to the serving pass [done]). A premature serve cannot have committed before a serve, so prevented should be True. Either this release path does not set prevented, or the commit for premature_serve is judged at injection or at the walk, which contradicts the "chopping it is a commit, picking up is not" principle.
  • C/B-cook (V7) — high — t46. During the search hold: "I'm just stepping over to the bottom counter first, and after that I'm heading up toward the counter along the top wall." The second clause announces a block step the body never reached (release t50). The valid flag and prevented=True were at least partly bought by the narration. (t41 "I'm heading down toward the counter along the bottom wall right now" was the current walk, which is fine.)
  • C/B-cook (V7, borderline) — medium — t79. During the omit_subgoal hold: "I'm heading over to the serving pass right now to serve this dish up." The kitchen did show the detour; the engine produced it.
  • D-assistant — medium — t0–t4. The novice's first direct question, "I'm ready to make the Caprese salad, what should I start with first?", gets (silent) at t4.
  • D-assistant — medium — t20. false_alarm: "You're searching around." The cook was on its long first walk to tomato source 1 (arrival t25).
  • D-assistant — medium — t73 (captured answer partly invented). assistant_said:plate: "Earlier, I told you to pick up a clean plate and keep it in hand for scooping, and if you ever set it down, it has to be on an empty counter only."   • D-assistant — medium — t88–t101. Cook t88: "Should I get the cheese from the crate on the bottom left or the one more towards the right?"; t90 does not answer; t95 "Use the cheese crate on the top wall of the kitchen (the one labeled CHEESE near the middle)". That is the tomato crate (cook: "isn't the crate near the top wall tomato?"), and the assistant conceded at t101.

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on the benchmark's validity.

  1. The cook narrates the mistake before its body does it (V7 leak). C/B-cook, high, 4 clear + 1 borderline.
  • Occurrences: ep1 t64 "grab an onion to chop up next"; ep4 t48 "going to grab some lettuce now"; ep8 t46 "after that I'm heading up toward the counter along the top wall"; ep6 t84 "heading over toward the serving window" (medium; the block had not reached the pass); ep8 t79 "to serve this dish up" (borderline).
  • Effect: in ep1 and ep8 the correct flag followed within 4 ticks and echoed the leaked words (early_correct, valid, prevented=True). In ep4 the assistant's only correct words about the lettuce (t53) came straight after the leak.
  • Diagnosis: harness/prompt. During a takeover the cook evidently knows the block's target objects and next commands. "Talk about your current action" is read as "describe the purpose of the current walk", which names the wrong item or the next step. Fix by withholding the upcoming block commands and the mistake's target from the cook's takeover prompt (give only the verb of the command in progress), or by filtering takeover utterances that name an object the body is not yet touching.
  1. Prevention and late grading contradict the timeline. F-grading, high, 2.
  • (a) ep8 t85: omit_subgoal (premature serve) accepted with no serve executed, yet recorded prevented=False (V9).
  • (b) ep7 t146: the right error named 10 ticks after the repeat_step release is graded false_alarm. The five late verdicts in this sample all arrived before the cook trashed the build; this is the only one that arrived after, which points to the late window being bound to the plate build instead of "30 ticks after release".
  • Diagnosis: harness grading rules for the premature_serve commit point and for the late window.
  1. failed_at / eval: labels are wrong. F-grading, medium (high if eval: feeds the score), 8/8.
  • failed_at=injection_skipped appears in 7/8 episodes: on the burned ep1, and on ep3, ep4, ep7 and ep8, where every sub-case was injected.
  • The one episode that does have skips and a score (ep2, score=0.474) prints failed_at=None.
  • eval: is empty in 7/8.
  • Diagnosis: the end-of-episode summary writer, possibly keyed on a stale flag or on the "archived at end" path.
  1. Anchors that fire on meaningless states. A-scheduling, high ×1 and medium ×3.
  • (a) ep2 t23, high: add_foreign with plate prefix ['steak'] matched a plate holding one RAW steak left by serve_raw. The cook had already queued the trash; the error committed 2 ticks after injection.
  • (b) serve_raw with prep_req={...: 0} fires at t0, before the cook's first command: ep2, ep4, ep5.
  • Diagnosis: the plate-prefix match ignores item state and ignores whether the build already carries a committed error; the serve_raw anchor derivation yields an all-zero prep requirement. Require prefix items in their recipe state, block injection on a build with a committed error, and give serve_raw a non-trivial anchor (e.g. the component's source visited or the plate fetched).
  1. In-flight block commands finish after release. A-scheduling (known issue), medium, 5 occurrences.
  • Occurrences: ep1 t75 (walk to onion source; ~10–17 ticks lost while rice smoked in the burned episode); ep3 t57 (walk to plates while meat smoked); ep5 t191 (wander); ep8 t56 (walk to counter (2,6)); ep8 t86 (walk to pass).
  • Side effect: the leftover walk is visible and gets flagged; ep1 t76 and ep8 t63 were both graded false_alarm.
  • Diagnosis: the release does not cancel the command in progress. At minimum, cancel walks, which are always safe to abort.
  1. The cook fakes compliance while its body is frozen in forget_next_step. B-cook (V4), medium, 3 lines in 2 episodes.
  • Occurrences: ep3 t74 "Yep, heading over to grab the cheese now.", ep3 t82 "On my way to scoop the cheese right now.", ep5 t271 "Plate is complete, taking it up to the pass now.". The body was idle each time.
  • Diagnosis: prompt. With no current action to describe, the model answers the assistant's instruction as if it were free. The takeover prompt for idle blocks should say explicitly "your body is not moving; say nothing".
  1. Voice-slot design and capture. A/F, medium.
  • (a) Proactive requests are never captured: answered tNone in all 5 (ep2 t199, ep3 t27, ep5 t248, ep6 t13, ep7 t21), including three with direct replies (ep5 t253 "I can do that", ep6 t21/t26, ep7 t25). This may be intentional (judge-graded), but it contradicts "the next spoken reply after the ask is captured".
  • (b) A question lands on the serve tick and cannot be answered (ep7 t256).
  • (c) Questions with false premises when asked: ep4 t118 "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" (meat is never chopped in this recipe; cheese not yet plated). ep3 t11 and ep4 t45 ask "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" while the only item was being loaded.
  • Diagnosis: case design plus anchors. Order questions should require both events to have happened; past_done:heat should require something to have been on heat for a while; two questions on the final-plate anchor collide with the serve.
  1. Takeovers and planning skills that break heat handling. A/harness, medium, 2 (both in ep4).
  • add_foreign injected between "put down" and "turn on" silently dropped the queued turn on, so the meat sat unlit t45–t103.
  • resolve_subgoal(assemble(meat)) returned "turn on" while the cook held a plate: t85 turn on [failed] — your hands must be EMPTY.
  • Diagnosis: queue drop at injection does not complete a started load→ignite pair; the assemble skill does not prepend park_plate before a turn on.
  1. Smoke warnings are graded by arrival tick. F-grading, medium, 2 in ep5.
  • t129: a true-when-issued smoke warning is graded false_alarm because the cook scooped on the arrival tick.
  • t219: a false-when-issued smoke warning is graded late because it fell inside a 30-tick window.
  • Diagnosis: verdicts for state claims should use the frame the assistant was looking at (issue tick), or allow a latency grace.
  1. Engine: second station reported on fire 10 ticks after COOKED. E, medium, 1 (ep1 t126: steak COOKED ~t116, BURNT/FIRE t126). Probably the end-of-episode fire marking all lit stations; the transcript should say so, or it breaks the 25 + 60 rule.
  1. Doubled navigation lines. Low, all 8 episodes: "go to X" followed one tick later by "go to the X" / "go to X (x,y)" (e.g. ep6 t187/t188, ep7 t36/t37). Harmless logging artefact.
  1. Assistant model behaviour (D, medium, recurring; these measure the assistant rather than defects of the harness):
  • Smoke noticed late or never: ep1 (+16 ticks, burned), ep2 (+17, the cook saw it first), ep3 (never mentioned), ep5 (+11), ep6 (+18).

What this assistant does well
